# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

## Project

"Gratitude Garden" is a static, dependency-free web project by Kenisha Gabriella Wijaya. Users answer reflection prompts and a flower is planted in a generative garden scene. There is no build system, no package manager, no tests, and no git repository. Every file is served as-is.

## THE PROJECT IS SHOWING TWO GARDENS ONLY, AND IT IS TEMPORARY

**Read this before anything else below, because most of this file describes pages the site no longer links to.** The feedback was that four destinations read as a feature party, so the project was narrowed to **The Personal Garden and The Shared Garden**. Everything else is still on disk, untouched, and unlinked.

| | state |
|---|---|
| The Personal Garden | live |
| The Shared Garden | live |
| The Bouquet Builder | **files intact, no link anywhere** |
| The Gardener's Studio and the gardener figure | **files intact, no link, not loaded** |
| The Guest Book | **files intact, no link** |
| Friends, inside `garden-account.js` | **behind `FRIENDS_OFF`** |
| Accounts, usernames, likes, comments, history | live, untouched |

- **NOTHING WAS DELETED AND NOTHING WAS REWRITTEN.** Every page still answers at its own URL, so you can open one to show somebody; nothing on the site points at it. That is the whole requirement: this has to come back in one revert.
- **THE GARDENS NEEDED ONLY THEIR SCRIPT TAGS REMOVED, and that is worth knowing before putting them back.** `bouquet-sketch.js`, `avatar-draw.js` and `garden-gardener.js` are gone from both garden pages, and no other line changed in either sketch. Every path that draws or pats a gardener already guarded on `window.AvatarDraw` and `window.GardenGardener`, so the sketches skip it; the shared garden's "who is this from" row asks `haveGardener()` and builds itself with two choices instead of three, falling back to `name`. **Restore the three tags and the gardener returns with the identity choice.**
- **`FRIENDS_OFF` IS ONE FLAG AT THE TOP OF `garden-account.js`.** The friends list exists almost entirely to send a bouquet, and with the builder unlinked a panel full of people to send nothing to is a feature pointing at a hole. The panel is who you are and a way out; its Account block is rebuilt in the early return rather than reached through the friends path, because the real one lives inside `loadFriends().then(...)` and that query would be a round trip for a list nothing shows. The chip's label follows the flag, since a button saying Friends that opens an account panel is worse than no label.
- **THE ACCOUNT STAYED, AND THE REASON IS THAT ALL OF IT IS GARDEN**: a username, the personal garden following you between devices, the once a day limit holding across them, and liking or replying in the meadow. None of that is friends.
- **WHAT THE LANDING PAGE LOST**: the bouquet card and the gardener band (commented out together in `#gardens`), all five perched gardeners, the three gardener and bouquet script tags with the block that painted them, the top nav's Bouquet entry, three footer links, and the closing section's third button. `.gardens-grid` went to **two columns**, which is the one CSS line that has to move with the card.
- **COPY THAT COUNTED TO THREE WAS REWRITTEN**, and it is easy to miss: the hero subtitle, the About lede and its three paragraphs, the About section title, the gardens eyebrow and description, all three Wisdom tips, and both gardens' Tips card line naming the corner icons. The gardens heading was "Reflect, share, give", where `give` was the bouquet and also the title's only `<em>`; it is "Reflect, and *share*" so the line still carries an emphasis for the wiggle to find.
- **THE BOUQUET FEATURE CARD IN ABOUT BECAME "Your history".** That grid is two by two so the count has to stay at four, and the history is a real part of the personal garden and the only part of it nothing else on the page mentions.
- **TWO DEAD LINKS SURVIVE ON PURPOSE**, in the `?hero=` drafts. Those are gated behind a query string, invisible to any ordinary visitor, and their painters already guard on `typeof renderBouquetCanvas === "function"`, so they simply do not paint. Leaving them is less to revert. **If a hero draft is ever promoted, those two objects and the three script tags have to come back together.**
- **The two painter blocks for the bouquet card and the hero objects are still in `index.html` and are inert**, each returning early on a missing element or a missing function. Same reasoning: less to undo.

## Running it

Serve the repo root over HTTP (relative paths and `localStorage` both need an origin, and opening `index.html` via `file://` breaks the garden pages):

```bash
cd "/Users/kenisha/Uni/EXPERIMENTAL/GRATITUDE GARDEN 2" && python3 -m http.server 8000
```

Then open `http://localhost:8000/`. Entry points: `/` (landing), `/personal-garden/personal-garden-index.html`, `/shared-garden/shared-garden-index.html`, `/bouquet/bouquet-index.html`.

Note the directory name contains a space, so quote paths in shell commands.

## Architecture

Four independent pages that share a visual language but **no code**, with one exception: `garden-music.js` at the root is loaded by all four (see "Sound" below). The eight species (tulip, rose, sunflower, **poppy**, lily, daisy, **peony**, lavender) are drawn by hand-ported copies of the same math in **seven** separate places. **The peony and the poppy replaced the lotus and the sakura**; both of those are still drawn everywhere and are hidden from every picker, for the reason in "The peony and the poppy" below. See "Keeping the flowers in sync" below:

| Page | Renderer | Persistence |
|---|---|---|
| `index.html` + `styles.css` | inline `<script>`, raw Canvas 2D | none |
| `personal-garden/` | p5.js global mode | `localStorage` |
| `shared-garden/` | p5.js + p5.party | realtime shared room |
| `bouquet/` | vanilla DOM + raw Canvas 2D | URL query string |

#### Keeping the flowers in sync

**A change to one flower's look must be applied in all seven sites.** They share no naming convention, so no single grep finds them all. The eight pickable species are tulip, rose, sunflower, **poppy**, lily, daisy, **peony** and lavender. FOUR more are drawn in all seven and hidden from every picker: an orchid, a chrysanthemum, and the **sakura and lotus** the poppy and the peony replaced. See "Two HIDDEN species" and "The peony and the poppy" below:

| # | Site | Signature (tulip) | Role |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `personal-garden-sketch.js:467+` | `drawTulipBloom(R, hue, sat, light)` | live canvas |
| 2 | `personal-garden-sketch.js:797+` | `drawTulipBloomOnGraphics(pg, R, hue, sat, light)` | preview buffer, via `drawPreviewFlower()` (`:758`) |
| 3 | `shared-garden-sketch.js:478+` | `drawTulipBloom(R, hue, sat, light)` | live canvas |
| 4 | `shared-garden-sketch.js:1230+` | `drawTulipPreview(pg, R, hue, sat, light)` | preview buffer, via `drawPreviewBloom()` (`:1477`) |
| 5 | `bouquet-sketch.js:163+` | `drawTulip(ctx, R, hue, sat, light)` | raw Canvas 2D, `ctx` first |
| 6 | `index.html:200+` | `drawTulip(R, hue, sat, light)` | hero meadow block |
| 7 | `index.html:882+` | `drawTulip(ctx, cx, cy, R, hue, sat, light)` | card-scene / catalogue block; its comment calls it "exact p5.js math" |

Both gardens carry a full 8-species preview family alongside the live one (rows 2 and 4), just named differently, so a grep for `OnGraphics` finds only the personal garden's.

**Sakura is named three different ways.** The species id is `sakura` everywhere in the *data* (mood maps, `speciesList`, saved flowers), but the drawing functions are:

- `drawSakura`: bouquet and both `index.html` blocks
- `drawCherryBloom` / `drawCherryBloomOnGraphics`: personal garden
- `drawCherryBloom` / `drawCherryBlossomPreview`: shared garden

So grepping `sakura` finds the data and never the rendering code.

#### THE HIBISCUS AND THE ORCHID REPLACED THE PEONY AND THE POPPY

**The eight pickable species are tulip, rose, sunflower, HIBISCUS, lily, daisy, ORCHID and lavender.** Everything in the section below about the peony and the poppy is still true of how they are DRAWN; they are simply no longer pickable. Six species are now retired or hidden: peony, poppy, sakura, lotus and chrysanthemum, with the orchid having come the other way.

- **THE TWO HALVES OF THIS COST WILDLY DIFFERENT AMOUNTS, and that is the useful thing to know.** The orchid had been drawn in all seven sites for its whole life as a hidden species, so turning it on was a DATA EDIT in the four hiding places and nothing else, exactly as the note on hidden species promised. The hibiscus did not exist anywhere and had to be drawn seven times.
- **THE HIBISCUS WAS DEFINED ONCE AND THE SEVEN COPIES GENERATED FROM IT**, the way the connected tulip was, so they cannot drift. The definition is a list of ellipse ops with a per-site emitter; it is not kept in the repo, because the seven copies are the artefact and a generator left lying about invites somebody to edit the output instead.
- **WHAT IT IS**: five broad petals at 72 degrees, 0.92 R wide on a 0.44 R ring so they overlap about 1.7 times and read as one five lobed shape; a vein down each; a two step throat; and the stamen column at FULL botanical length, out to 1.31 R. **The throat names it at garden size and the column names it in the guide**, which is the split that lets one drawing serve a 30px bloom and a 170px one.
- **THE COLUMN SURVIVES AT GARDEN SIZE ONLY BECAUSE OF THE STIGMA.** A bare 1.3 R line at R 30 is a one pixel stroke that reads as a stray mark; the five lobed stigma at the tip gives it a terminus, so it reads as a thing pointing rather than as a scratch. **If the stigma is ever removed, the column has to go with it.**
- **THE ANTHERS ARE A FIXED GOLD**, not the flower's own hue, which is the daisy's precedent and the only one in the set: a part that stays the same colour whatever the sender picks.
- **`BLOOM_BOX` AND `HALO_BOX` DISAGREE MORE FOR THIS SPECIES THAN FOR ANY OTHER, on purpose.** Measured at R 100 the painted box is `l -0.95, r 1.13, t -0.97, b 0.92`, and the whole of that extra width is the column. `BLOOM_BOX` records it truthfully at `[-0.03, 1.13, 0.95]`. `HALO_BOX` is fitted to the PETALS alone at `[0.00, 0.89, 0.89]`, measured radially over 720 directions with the column's own sector skipped: 0.74 at the notch, 0.98 at the tip. **An ellipse stretched to cover the column would be the lavender's fog again**, which is the same reasoning that sizes the peony's halo to its notch.
- **THE RING SURVIVED THE SWAP WITH ONE THING TO WATCH.** The hibiscus is red at 352 and the rose is red at 350, which is **two degrees apart and the closest pair the wheel has ever carried**. They sit at opposite ends of it, so they are never neighbours and every ADJACENT step still climbs by at least 18 degrees. Moving the hibiscus off red was refused: a hibiscus is red, and the ring is a way of showing eight flowers rather than a promise about the spectrum.
- **ALL EIGHT MEANINGS MOVED, not just the two new ones**, because the set was re-grounded against gratitude research with a domain per flower. Rose love and connection, sunflower joy and warmth, orchid strength and resilience, tulip hope and new beginnings, lavender calm and safety, daisy simplicity and contentment, lily trust and faith, hibiscus presence and impermanence. **No word does duty for two of the eight**, which was checked: the sunflower moving off "resilience" is what freed that word for the orchid.
- **THE MOOD MAPS FOLLOW THE DOMAINS.** `stressed` and `work/school` go to the orchid, whose domain is growth after hardship; `grateful` and `a small joy` go to the hibiscus, whose domain is what is fleeting, and a small joy is exactly that. `tired` keeps the lily although its meaning moved from rest to faith, because nothing else was free.
- **Verified by exhausting the space rather than reading the maps**, which is this project's rule for every swap: 392 combinations of rating, shaper and journal text run through `chooseFlowerForMood`, **all eight pickable species reachable, not one retired species ever returned**, and every entry in `flowerMeanings` still carrying an ARRAY `keywords` so nothing can throw.
- **The orchid's chip colour changed with its status.** It was `--lightsky` while it was hidden and nothing ever showed it; a pickable orchid gets an orchid purple.
- **A CACHED SKETCH WASTED A CHECK AGAIN**, on the shared garden's picker this time. `fetch(url, {cache:"reload"})` on the script followed by a reload is the fix, and reading the live value back is the only way to know.

##### THE WHOLE ORCHID IS TRANSCRIBED NOW, and the last peonies are gone

The swap above left the orchid drawn exactly as it had been while it was
hidden, and the thing nobody had looked at was its CENTRE. It took three goes
and the last one was done by transcribing a drawing rather than designing a
shape. Separately, peony and poppy were out of every picker but still showing
in five places the pickers do not reach.

- **GO ONE: THREE LOBES ALL POINTING DOWNWARDS**, at 62, 118 and 90 degrees,
  so every part of the centre painted BELOW the origin: measured at R 150 its
  centre of mass sat at +0.254 Ro against a flower whose own painted middle is
  at -0.177 Ro, which is 52 percent of the way from the middle to the bottom
  edge. It read as a pendant hanging off the petals rather than as the middle
  of the flower.
- **GO TWO: TWO ARMS THAT RISE, which fixed the position and not the shape.**
  Anchoring the side lobes at the origin and tilting them 22 degrees off
  vertical does carry magenta ABOVE the junction where the two broad petals
  meet, and the centre of mass went from +0.254 Ro to +0.057. What two
  tapering lobes with a gap between them read as is a pair of horns.
- **GO THREE: ONE CLOSED RING with the column pale inside it.** Round, and
  a doughnut: it has no top, no bottom and no parts, where the thing it is
  drawing is plainly a little assembly stacked one part on another.
- **GO FOUR: FOUR PARTS off a pencil drawing.** Anther cap, pale column, the
  lip's side lobes closed round it as one mass, and the midlobe below with its
  two corners turned up. Right in its parts and still wrong as a picture: the
  mass was a soft egg where the reference has a hard little assembly, and at
  garden size it read as a blob with a dot on it.
- **GO FIVE: THE WHOLE FLOWER OFF A FLAT DRAWING, not the centre alone**, and
  that is what finally fixed the PETALS. Every round before it had tuned the
  centre inside petals nobody had looked at, which is most of why there were
  so many. The two broad petals are far rounder and swung far more horizontal
  than ours were, at **22 degrees off horizontal** against the old 50, with
  the lower sepals widened to match. **The petals have not moved since** and
  nothing below is about them.
- **GO SIX, AND THE ONE THAT HOLDS: THE ARMS ARE ONE HORSESHOE, NOT TWO
  SHAPES.** Go five drew them as two separate lobes either side of a column
  and it was wrong three ways at once, all of them the same mistake: a fat
  bezier lobe is a club, widest near its TIP, so the pair merged into one
  blocky hood at the top, the column vanished into it, and the whole centre
  read as a bat or a cape rather than as a flower's middle. Drawn as two slim
  upright ellipses instead it read as three vertical bars, which is worse. The
  reference's arms are ONE shape that curls in round the cap on both sides.
- **IT IS A THICK STROKED ARC WITH ROUND CAPS, and that is the first stroked
  thing in the whole set of twelve.** `arc` in p5, `ctx.ellipse` with start
  and stop angles on a raw canvas, a `lineWidth` of `0.10 Ro` and a **92
  degree gap left at the top** for the anther cap to sit in. Nothing else can
  make a crescent here: a ring needs its middle cut out, and these sketches
  have no compositing, only fills on whatever colour the petals happen to be.
- **THE GAP IS THE WHOLE CONTROL.** At 64 degrees the tips close over the cap
  and the centre is a doughnut again; at 92 they stand either side of it like
  a pair of horns, which is the reference. **The cap stays the topmost thing
  on the flower's middle**: it is what the horseshoe is cradling, and tips
  rising past it turn the group back into a V.
- **TWO p5 TRAPS, AND BOTH BITE SILENTLY.** The stroke is wrapped in
  `push()`/`pop()`, since `strokeWeight` and `strokeCap` are global state and
  a species function that leaks them changes every stroke drawn after it in
  that frame. And the constant is the GLOBAL `ROUND`, never `pg.ROUND`: a
  `p5.Graphics` carries the drawing methods and not the constants, so
  `pg.strokeCap(pg.ROUND)` passes `undefined` and the caps come out square on
  the preview family alone, which is the copy nobody looks at first.
- **THE HORSESHOE CHANGED NO TABLE, and that was checked rather than assumed.**
  A stroke reaches half its width past its own path, so it could have: the arc
  sits at `0.155 Ro` with a `0.05 Ro` overhang, which is nowhere near the
  petals. The painted box came back identical at `l -1.21, r 1.20, t -1.24,
  b 0.96`, so every table go five moved stayed where go five put it.
- **GO FIVE'S PETALS DID MOVE EVERYTHING, though.**
  That is the difference between this round and the four before it, which were
  all interior and left every table standing. The painted box went from
  `l -1.04, r 1.03, t -1.28, b 0.83` to **`l -1.21, r 1.20, t -1.24, b 0.96`**,
  so `BLOOM_METRICS.orchid`, `BLOOM_BOX.orchid` and `HALO_BOX.orchid` in BOTH
  gardens, the ring's own `cy` (`size*0.568` to **`size*0.542`**) and the
  avatar hood all moved with it. Checked after: the p5 preview family and the
  raw canvas family agree to a pixel, and all eight ring blooms still land
  between 0.478 and 0.500 of their slot.
- **THE ORCHID IS NOW WIDER THAN IT IS TALL, and that is what cost the avatar
  hood its single radius.** It spans 1.21 R across and 1.10 down, where it
  used to be 1.04 by 1.06, so no one `R` can land its tips at 84 on all three
  sides any more. The sides win, since the bonnet's WIDTH is what reads as a
  hood: `R: 70, dy: 10` puts them at 84.4 with the top and bottom at about 77.
- **THE CORNERS ARE WHAT NAME IT, and they have to be SMALL.** A phalaenopsis
  midlobe carries two antennae that turn up at its tip, and that anchor shape
  is the one part of the flower nothing else in the set has. Drawn at 0.18 Ro
  and near vertical they read as **a cat's face**, which is what three earlier
  lips did; at `0.12 Ro` long, `0.042 Ro` wide and 34 degrees off vertical
  they read as corners of the lip they belong to.
- **THE CAP IS A THIRD TONE, AND IT WAS MEASURED RATHER THAN CHOSEN.** The
  deep is `light - 15` and the sepals behind it are `light + 13`, so a cap
  anywhere near the petals disappears into them: at `sat * 0.95, light + 2` it
  was invisible at R 46 and a scan down the centre line could not tell it from
  the petal above. It is `sat * 1.05, light - 4`, which leaves three tones
  that separate, and it stays a pink cap rather than a second dark blob, which
  is what a fully deep one looked like.
- **THE COLUMN HAD TO GAIN COLOUR.** At `sat * 0.35, light + 26` it is
  effectively white, and ringed by deep magenta it read as a HOLE punched in
  the flower. It is `sat * 0.60, light + 20`, a soft pink that still reads as
  the palest thing on the bloom.
- **`lobe` CARRIES `oy` AND `ox` NOW, and they translate in FLOWER space
  before the rotate**, so a part can be anchored away from the origin. The
  midlobe's corners need both. The catalogue copy folds them into its own
  `cx, cy` translate.
- **NOTHING HAD TO BE RE-MEASURED THROUGH GOES TWO, THREE AND FOUR, and that
  was checked rather than assumed each time.** All three were interior, so the
  painted box came back identical every time at `l -1.04, r 1.03, t -1.28,
  b 0.83`. Go five moved the petals and moved everything with them. **Measure
  the box on any change at all**, interior or not; it is one run, and it is
  the difference between knowing and hoping.
- **`lobe` BRIEFLY GAINED AN `oy` AND HAS LOST IT AGAIN.** The arms had to be
  anchored away from the origin; the ring is ellipses and needs no such thing,
  so the parameter and its `translate` are gone from all seven rather than
  left sitting dead. The catalogue copy is the one that folded it into its own
  `cx, cy` translate, which is the thing to know if it ever comes back.
- **THE SEVEN COPIES WERE GENERATED FROM ONE DEFINITION**, the way the
  connected tulip and the hibiscus were, with one emitter per call style:
  p5 globals, p5 graphics, the bouquet's `ctx`, and `index.html`'s two compact
  blocks. Note that the two p5 families and the bouquet take ellipse
  DIAMETERS and the two `index.html` blocks take RADII.
- **THE RING NEEDED THE ORCHID'S ORIGIN MOVED, and it is the biggest shift of
  the eight.** The orchid paints 1.28 R above its origin and 0.83 below, so
  its middle sits 0.23 R high, which at the ring's R of 0.30 size is 14px up a
  210px slot. `cy` is `size*0.568` for it and `size*0.508` for the hibiscus.
  Verified by reading every ring canvas back: all eight painted centres now
  land between 0.478 and 0.500 of their slot.
- **WHERE THE LAST PEONIES WERE**, and none of them was a picker: the shared
  garden card's middle bloom on the landing page (putu's, the purple of its
  three), the hero drafts' palette and the two journal objects that use it,
  both example bouquets, the avatar studio's flower list and two of its five
  templates, and the modal's fallback SVGs.
- **THE DRAWING CODE STAYS. Retired, not deleted, as before.** A peony planted
  before the swap still draws and a bouquet link naming one still opens, which
  is the rule the swap was made under and the reason the ring's own peony
  branch was kept beside the two new ones.
- **THE HIBISCUS CANNOT RING A FACE, AND IT IS THE THIRD SPECIES THAT CANNOT.**
  As an avatar hood at R 88 the petals landed at 84 with everything else and
  the bonnet looked right; what it also did was send the stamen column, at
  1.13 R the longest thing any of the twelve paints, out from behind the head
  and down across the shoulder, ending in its stigma over the arm. It reads as
  a stray object laid on the figure. Dropping the column would mean a new
  argument in all seven copies of `drawHibiscus`, and the column is what names
  the species in the guide, so the hibiscus SPROUTS instead, which is the same
  answer the tulip and the lavender already get. The orchid rings the face at
  R 80 with `dy` 18, which lands every tip within a pixel of 84.
- **THE SPROUT SENTENCE MOVED ONTO THE TABLE THAT DECIDES THE DRAWING.** The
  picker printed one line for every sprout, "has no ring of petals to sit
  inside", and with three sprouts that is now wrong for whichever it was not
  written for: the hibiscus HAS a ring of petals and sprouts for the column.
  Each sprout entry in `HOOD` carries its own `note`, and the panel's own
  heading went from six ringing the face to five.

#### The peony and the poppy

**THE LOTUS AND THE SAKURA ARE OUT OF THE PICKERS. The peony took the lotus's place and the poppy took the sakura's**, with their meanings, keywords, mood mappings, hues, chips and catalogue tiles. Nothing else about the eight moved.

- **The reason is that neither retired species is a flower on a stem.** A lotus grows out of water and a cherry blossom grows on a branch, which is why the 3D exploration had to give one its own pool and the other a whole tree before it gave up and went back to stems. They are also exactly the two the florist research had already found could not be bought as cut stems, so the same pair fails for the same underlying reason in two unrelated places.
- **RETIRED, NOT DELETED, and the difference is load bearing.** Both keep their drawing functions in all seven sites, their rows in `LEGACY_SPECIES` and `SPECIES_MAP`, their `BLOOM_BOX` and `BLOOM_METRICS` entries and their origin nudges. Every bouquet link ever shared names its species, and every flower already planted carries its own, so a link from before the swap still opens and a lotus planted last year still draws and still says something when it is tapped. **Do not delete any of it.**
- **Their keyword lists are EMPTY, never missing**, with the real list parked beside them as `keywordsHidden`, which is the same rule the orchid and the chrysanthemum already follow and for the same reason: `chooseFlowerForMood` reads `.keywords` off entries in `flowerMeanings` and a removed field throws the moment anyone writes a journal entry.
- **Where the swap actually happens** is the same four places the hidden pair are hidden in: `SPECIES` against `LEGACY_SPECIES` in `bouquet-sketch.js`, `speciesList` in `shared-garden-sketch.js`, `ratingFlowerMap` / `shaperFlowerMap` / the `votes` object in `personal-garden-sketch.js`, and the catalogue tile markup in `index.html`. Everything else already carries all twelve.
- **The mood maps are a straight substitution and it holds semantically.** `stressed` and `work/school` pointed at the lotus for its resilience and now point at the peony for the same thing; `grateful` and `a small joy` pointed at the sakura for its reflection and now point at the poppy, which carries reflection in its own meaning. Nothing else in either map moved, so the vote arithmetic is untouched.
- **Verified by exhausting the space, not by reading the maps**: all 448 combinations of rating, shaper and journal text were run through `chooseFlowerForMood`, only the eight pickable species ever come out, none of the four hidden ones ever does, and both new species are reachable.

**THE PEONY IS THREE RINGS AND A PALE CENTRE, ported from the reference drawing rather than invented.** Ten outer petals at 36 degrees alternating two lengths and two tones, six shorter inner petals, and a small warm centre. A peony petal is nearly as wide as it is long, which is what separates it from the daisy's sixteen narrow ones, and the pale centre is what separates it from the rose, whose middle is a spiral of ever smaller petals in the flower's own colour.

- **IT IS DELIBERATELY IRREGULAR and its painted box is NOT symmetric.** Measured at R 100: left -0.96, right 0.95, top -1.00, bottom 0.84, so its centre sits 0.08 R above its origin. That is recorded rather than corrected, in `BLOOM_BOX` as `[-0.08, 0.96, 0.92]`, in `BLOOM_METRICS`, and as the ring's own `cy = size*0.525`. Two rings of five were tried at one point and made no difference at all, since five long petals at 72 degrees land in the same places whether they are written as one loop or two.

**THE POPPY IS EIGHT PETALS IN TWO LAYERS AND A DARK EYE.** Four deep petals on the axes and four brighter ones turned 45 degrees over them, then an eye that goes nearly black, three ribs across it and six stamen dots.

- **Four petals alone read as a cross with gaps in it.** The first version was the reference's earlier four petal drawing and it read as a symbol rather than a flower; the second layer fills the gaps and the two tones are what keep the layers apart when a sender re-colours the whole bloom.
- **The eye keeps a little of the flower's own hue** rather than being flat black, or a re-coloured poppy reads as a sticker stuck on a bloom. It is the only near black centre in the set and the poppy is the only eight petalled bloom, which is what makes it the easiest of the eight to name with the colour removed.
- Its painted box is symmetric and exactly 1.00 R.

**THE MEANINGS WERE AUDITED ACROSS ALL FIVE PLACES THEY APPEAR, and two of the eight disagreed with themselves before the swap ever happened.** A species' wording lives in `SPECIES` in the bouquet, `speciesList` in the shared garden, `flowerMeanings` in the personal garden, `flowerData` on the landing page and the catalogue tile's own `flower-mood` line. The rule has always been that one flower says one thing everywhere; these two did not.

- **The daisy was "Simplicity and joy" in three places and "Simplicity and lightness" in two.** It is joy everywhere now, which is also the better fit for a flower whose settled meaning is cheerfulness and innocence.
- **The lily was "Peace and restoration" in four places and "Peace and rest" in the bouquet.** It is restoration everywhere now.
- **The peony is "Honour and strength"**, not the "Honour and resilience" it arrived with. Resilience was already the sunflower's, and one word doing duty for two of eight is the thing this audit exists to catch. Honour and strength is also the better founded pair: the peony is the flower of honour and nobility in Chinese symbolism and of bravery in Japanese.
- **The sunflower keeps "Hope and resilience"** and that was checked rather than assumed. Hope is the common modern reading, and resilience is a real contemporary association rather than an invention. With the peony moved off the word, nothing is doubled.
- The other five were checked and left alone: rose love and depth, tulip renewal and change, lavender calm and safety, poppy memory and reflection, daisy as above. **Re-run the comparison across all five places if any wording is ever touched.**

**A NEW SPECIES HAS TO LAND INSIDE THE PALETTE'S EXISTING RANGE, on every page, and the poppy did not.** Each page carries its own saturation and lightness family and they are narrower than they look:

| | saturation | lightness |
|---|---|---|
| bouquet `SPECIES` | 40 to 82 | 52 to 70 |
| both gardens, `defaultSat` / `defaultLight` | 40 to 60 | 55 to 70 |
| the landing ring and its tiles | 42 to 62 | 55 to 70 |
| the hero meadow | 42 to 62 | 55 to 76 |

The poppy arrived at 68/54, 74/52 and 66/56, taken from the reference drawing's own scarlet, and it was the loudest thing on a page of pastels: in the ring it read as a sticker on a wheel of soft colour. It is 58 to 62 saturation and 56 to 58 lightness now, which keeps it plainly red while sitting in the family. **The hue was not what made it shout, the saturation was**, and the dark eye is what names the flower anyway.

**The seven copies were checked against each other rather than assumed**: the p5 preview family and the raw canvas family agree to within half a pixel at R 100 on both new species.

**THE COLOUR WHEEL ON THE LANDING PAGE LOST ITS GREEN.** The lotus had been pushed to hue 140 precisely because it was the one of three pinks that could go green, and it was filling the only stretch of the ring with nothing in it. A peony is pink and a poppy is red, so the yellow to cyan arc is now a 142 degree gap where it was 92. Only two hues moved for the swap: the daisy went 25 to 28 to keep 20 degrees clear of the poppy, and the two new flowers took the pink and red ends. Moving the lily out to about 150 and calling it a green white one would have balanced the spacing and taken a second liberty with a species' colour to prop up a device; the ring is a way of showing eight flowers, not a promise about the spectrum.

**THE TULIP IS ONE CONNECTED SHAPE in all seven sites.** It was three slightly see-through pieces (a notched crown, a round bowl, a floating pale oval), which showed a darker band where they overlapped and a step where the crown's sides met the bowl. It is now a solid back silhouette rising to two outer tips and a solid front petal from the base to the centre tip, and NOTHING ELSE. The copies were generated from one set of shapes, so they cannot drift. `BLOOM_BOX` in both gardens and `BLOOM_METRICS` in `index.html` were re-measured for it; the ring's per species origin offset for the tulip (`cy = size*0.465`) was not, and the shift is about a pixel.

**THE TULIP'S HIGHLIGHT IS GONE, from all seven sites, and it is the reason the tulip used to look unlike the rest.** It was a thin crescent down the LEFT of the front petal at `sat * 0.8, light + 14` and **alpha 0.32**, and it was two kinds of odd at once: the only translucent fill anywhere in the set, and the only asymmetric marking on any of the twelve species. At the size a garden draws a bloom it is about a pixel wide, so it read as a crease or a scratch rather than as shading.

- **Every other species builds depth from SOLID fills at a different lightness**, which is the idiom it broke. The rose is concentric circles from `light - 10` to `+10`, the poppy and the peony a solid centre, the lily a solid throat, the sunflower a solid disc. Petal alphas across the set run 0.85 to 1.0; nothing else is anywhere near 0.32.
- **It was also the WEAKEST highlight in the set, which is the argument against keeping it.** Measured on the painted pixels at the planter's `light 66`, the tulip's 95th percentile lightness was **68.2**, the lowest of all eight, where every other species reaches 73.9 to 82. So it was paying the cost of an odd marking without buying the depth one.
- **The tulip still reads, because the two-tone step does all the work**: a back silhouette at `light - 7` behind a front petal at `light`. Checked at R 30, 38, 46 and 60.
- **Nothing had to be re-measured.** The highlight sat inside the front petal, so the painted box is unchanged at 0.07, 0.60, 0.83 against the recorded 0.08, 0.61, 0.84, which is the same tolerance as before. `BLOOM_BOX`, `HALO_BOX` and `BLOOM_METRICS` all still hold.
- **A comment stands where each one was**, in all seven, so it does not get put back. Two alternatives were offered and turned down: mirroring the sliver so the petal curls both sides, and broadening it into soft shading. Both keep interior line work that no other species has.

#### Two HIDDEN species: orchid and chrysanthemum

**FOUR species are drawn everywhere and pickable nowhere**: `orchid` and `chrysanth`, which were never pickable, and `sakura` and `lotus`, which were until the peony and the poppy replaced them. All four are fully drawn in all seven sites and resolvable everywhere, and appear in **no picker, no catalogue and no mood map**. Nothing can plant one. They exist so the work is not lost, so old links and old flowers still render, and so turning any of them on is a data edit rather than a drawing job. The section below was written about the first two and applies to all four.

- **Where the hiding actually happens**, and it is only these four places. Everything else already handles all twelve: `SPECIES` vs `LEGACY_SPECIES` in `bouquet-sketch.js` (move the entries between the two lists); `speciesList` in `shared-garden-sketch.js`; `ratingFlowerMap`, `shaperFlowerMap` and the `votes` object in `personal-garden-sketch.js`, plus empty `keywords` arrays on the two hidden entries in `flowerMeanings`; and the catalogue tile markup in `index.html`. The drawing functions, `SPECIES_MAP`, `drawSpeciesBloom`, `bloomFns`, `flowerData`, `chipClasses`, the chip colours in `styles.css`, `defaultSat` / `defaultLight` / `speciesPetalCount` / `speciesShapeCfg` and every dispatcher already carry all twelve.
- **The hidden entries keep an EMPTY `keywords` array, never a missing one.** `chooseFlowerForMood` reads `.keywords` off entries in `flowerMeanings`, so removing or renaming the field throws the moment anyone writes a journal entry. The real lists are parked beside them as `keywordsHidden`, ready to swap back. That loop also iterates `votes` rather than `flowerMeanings`, so a keyword hit on a hidden species cannot write `votes[undefined]` and poison the result.
- **Verified by exhausting the space, not by reading the maps**: all 280 combinations of rating, shaper and journal text were run through `chooseFlowerForMood`, and only the original eight ever come out.
- **Every hidden species still draws**, which is deliberate: `SPECIES_MAP` resolves them, so a share link that names one opens instead of erroring, and the sync table above stays true.
- **The orchid is a phalaenopsis face on**: three sepals behind, two broad round petals in front swung nearly horizontal, and in the middle a pale anther cap crowning two upright arms with the column between them, all flaring into the midlobe below. That centre is what makes it read as an orchid rather than a generic five petalled bloom, and the whole flower was rebuilt five times; see "THE WHOLE ORCHID IS TRANSCRIBED NOW" above. It needs no label offset and no `bloomReach` entry, but it DOES need an origin nudge in the landing page's ring, since it paints 1.28 R above its origin and 0.83 below.
- **The chrysanthemum is a POMPOM and that is load bearing.** A chrysanthemum is in the same family as the daisy, and drawn as a single flat bloom the two tiles are the same picture twice. Four concentric rings of short petals lightening inward reads as a ball; the daisy is one flat ring of sixteen. Checked with colour removed, which is the case that matters since a bouquet sender can set any species to any hue.
- **Both were sized by measurement.** As a multiple of R the set runs tulip 0.61, lily 0.92, rose and daisy 0.98, lavender 0.39 wide by 1.06 tall, sunflower 1.33. The chrysanthemum lands at 1.17 and needs no scaling if it is ever turned on. The orchid was 1.02 and is **1.21** since it was redrawn from the flat reference, which puts it between the daisy and the sunflower rather than beside the rose. A spider chrysanthemum was drawn and rejected: unmistakable, but it reached 1.9R, read as tangled at garden size, and a spiky flower is the wrong shape for a meaning about quiet reflection.

#### Florist sourcing, RESEARCH ONLY, not applied to the code

This exists because the project may be made with real florists. **None of it is reflected in the eight above**, which are unchanged. The plan is for a florist orderable set to be its own separate thing for the real bouquet, not a change to the garden.

- **Six of the eight are easy**: rose, tulip, sunflower, lily and lavender are year round florist staples, and daisy is too once you say which one you mean.
- **Sakura cannot be ordered at all.** Cherry blossom sells as cut branches for a few weeks in early spring, not as bouquet stems, and drops petals within days.
- **Lotus is nearly as hard.** Fresh cut lotus is an expensive mid to late summer item moving mostly through Asian wholesale markets, and the blooms open and close on a daily cycle then collapse. Dried lotus *pods* are common year round, but that is the seed head, not the flower.
- **"Daisy" is ambiguous rather than hard.** A florist hears it and reaches for a gerbera; the drawing is a marguerite. Say which before anyone quotes.
- **Two smaller notes**: fresh lavender is summer only in most markets, dried is year round; and lily pollen stains and is toxic to cats, so florists strip the anthers for gift work.
- **This research is what the peony and the poppy eventually came out of.** The swap that shipped is not the orchid and the chrysanthemum below; it is the peony and the poppy, chosen by the author, and the two stand ins here stayed hidden. What the research got right was the PAIR: the lotus and the sakura are the two that fail, and they fail in the 3D exploration for the same underlying reason.
- **The two stand ins already drawn** are the orchid, for lotus, and the chrysanthemum, for sakura. Orchid is year round from Thailand and the Netherlands with two to three weeks of vase life; its meaning is looser than lotus's, closer to grace and endurance than to rising out of hardship, and it crowds the rose's "love and depth" a little. Chrysanthemum is a top three global cut flower, cheap and year round, and carries the reflection meaning honestly through the Chinese Four Gentlemen, where it is the flower of autumn and of the scholar in contemplation. **One warning if this ever ships to Europe:** in France, Italy, Spain, Belgium and Poland the chrysanthemum is a grave flower for All Saints' Day and a bouquet of them reads as a serious misstep. No such association in Indonesia, the UK, the US or Australia, where it is a Mother's Day flower.

### Personal garden (`personal-garden/personal-garden-sketch.js`, ~2300 lines)

Single-file p5 global-mode sketch. The entire UI is built imperatively in `buildUI()` with p5.dom (`createDiv`/`createButton`/`createElement`) and inline `.style()` calls into `#ui-root`. The HTML file is nearly empty, so **UI changes go in the JS, not the HTML**.

**The shared garden's `.gg-card` is padded `34px 28px 26px`.** At 20px a 40px title sat on the card's own edge. The flower grid and the colour step set their padding inline and keep their own.

**`.gg-title` and `.gg-sub` carry their own margins.** Left to the browser's h2 and p defaults there were 33px between a title and the line under it and only 18px between that line and the field it introduced, which reads as the line belonging to nothing. They are 10px and 26px now. Screens that set margins inline, the shared garden's select card among them, are unaffected, so check for an inline value before changing these.

One exception: `personal-garden-style.css` is not the near-empty file it looks like (401 lines of `.gg-card`, focus states, ~22 classes and 11 ID rules), and it ends with a `@media (max-width: 768px)` block (`:318`) that restyles `.gg-tile`, `.gg-btn`, `.gg-title`, `#tips-card` and others, some with `!important`. `buildUI()` sets those same properties inline. Inline usually wins, but not against `!important`, so **a mobile-only layout bug can come from either file; check the 768px block before chasing it through the JS**. `shared-garden-style.css` has the same shape (`:299`).

**Both garden stylesheets now END with a phone block, and every rule in it carries `!important`.** That is not defensiveness: `buildUI()` sets the very same properties inline with p5.dom `.style()` calls, and inline beats a stylesheet on everything except `!important`. It is the only way a stylesheet can reach a p5-built interface at all. **Anything added to `buildUI()` with an inline size needs a line here too**, or it stays at its desktop size on a phone while everything around it shrinks.

- The figures, the same in both files: `.gg-card` padding `22px 18px 20px` at `width: min(94vw, 460px)`; headings 25px; `.gg-sub` and `p` 15px; `.gg-btn` and `.gg-back` 16px with a `min-height: 46px`; `.gg-grid` two columns; `.gg-tile` a 150px minimum; `.preview-holder` 120 by 86; `#tips-card h3` 17px; `#daily-note` 14px.
- **Inputs are 16px and that is a hard floor, not a preference.** iOS zooms the whole page in when a focused field is under 16px, and it does not zoom back out. Every other size on the phone can be tuned; this one cannot go down.
- **A 46px minimum on the buttons is the touch target**, not the type size. A 16px label in a box that fits it exactly is about 30px tall, which is under what a thumb reliably hits.
- **The block sits at the END of the file**, like the bouquet's. A later rule beats an earlier one at equal specificity, so a phone override written above the base declaration it is overriding does nothing at all, silently. That trap has now been hit on three separate stylesheets in this project.

- Screen flow is a `step` string (`prompt1` → `prompt2` → `prompt3` → `flowerPreview` → `garden`) that toggles `display` on the wrapper divs and gates canvas `pointer-events`. Two functions do this: `showStep(s)` (`:2162`) and `updateScreen()` (`:1611`), which duplicate the show/hide logic with different reset behaviour, so check which one your code path actually calls. `step` starts at `"prompt1"` here, but `"landing"` in the shared garden.
- `chooseFlowerForMood()` picks the species by weighted vote: mood rating +4 (`ratingFlowerMap`), "what shaped your day" +3 (`shaperFlowerMap`), +2 per keyword hit from `flowerMeanings[species].keywords` against the journal text. `flowerMeanings` is also the source of the tooltip meaning and supportive quote.
- `addFlower()` fills three depth layers (`front`, `mid`, `back`, `ROW_CAPACITY = 8` each) and picks x by sampling 80 candidates and keeping the one with the most clearance from existing blooms.
- Flowers store **normalized** coordinates (`xNorm`, `stemNorm`, `sizeNorm`, relative to `BASE_W`/`BASE_H` = 1440×900). `updateResponsiveFlowerLayout()` re-derives pixel values on every resize and scales the whole garden down if the tallest bloom would exceed `height * 0.32`.
- `draw()` paints strictly back-to-front: sky → clouds → back-layer flowers → `drawHillsBack()` → mid flowers → `drawHillsFront()` → ground → front flowers → `drawForegroundHill()`. The hills are drawn *after* their layer's flowers so they occlude the stem bases.
**Every bloom casts ONE soft drop shadow, and it is legibility rather than style.** The ground runs `#a9d9cf`, `#8fcfbe` and `#7ec4b4`, all pale teals, and the planter picks the hue freely, so a pale teal or pale blue bloom sat on that ground at almost no contrast and disappeared into the grass. `bloomShadow(f, R)` runs before the species dispatch inside `drawBloom`, in **both** gardens.

- **A shadow was chosen over an outline or a floor on lightness** because it works whatever hue is picked, and the other two fight the choice the person just made.
- **The first version let EVERY PETAL cast one and it was wrong.** It reads as petal by petal shading rather than as a flower standing on grass, because a petal's shadow falls on the petals around it. Canvas cannot shadow a group of shapes as one.
- **An exact silhouette is not available to these sketches.** It would mean rendering the bloom to an offscreen buffer, and the species functions draw through p5's globals; the only parallel families that take a buffer are the PREVIEW ones, which are separate copies, so the outline under the flower would not be the flower's own.
- **So it is ONE ellipse per bloom, drawn far off canvas and brought back purely as its own shadow.** That is what makes it a single soft shape with one alpha and no seams. `BLOOM_BOX` holds `[centre y, half width, half height]` as multiples of R, **measured** by rendering each species alone and reading back its painted bounding box, not guessed. Lotus and lavender have a negative centre y because `bloomOriginNudge` pushes their origin down and they grow upward; a shadow centred on the origin sat below the flower. **`BLOOM_BOX` no longer feeds the halo**: it is still the true painted box, and `HALO_BOX` beside it is the ellipse the shadow is drawn from. See "The halo was too harsh" below.
- **Canvas shadow offsets and blur are NOT touched by the transform.** p5 scales `drawingContext` by the pixel density, so both have to be multiplied by `pixelDensity()` by hand, or the shadow lands in the wrong place and comes out half as soft as asked for.
- **`SHADOW_FIT` is 0.82**, so the flower overhangs its own shadow rather than sitting inside a dark halo exactly its own size. It multiplies `HALO_BOX`, not `BLOOM_BOX`. It was 0.90; see "The halo was too harsh" below for why it came down and why it must not go lower.
- **It is CENTRED on the bloom and light, not dropped below it.** A shadow cast down and to the side says the light is low and hard, which is the wrong weather for a pastel garden and read as a heavy smudge under every flower. Centred and soft it is a halo that separates the bloom from the grass and says nothing about the sun, which is what this needed to do in the first place.
- **Being centred costs contrast, and that was the trade accepted.** At `rgba(18,62,56,0.30)` offset down, the mean gain at the bloom edge was 0.21 and the average darkening 12 out of 255. At `0.18` centred it is 0.09 and 8.8. All eight species still gain: daisy 1.67 to 1.78, rose 1.65 to 1.77, tulip 1.87 to 1.98, sakura 1.35 to 1.44. **If it ever needs to be stronger, raise the alpha before putting the offset back.**

- Growth is time-based per flower (`GROWTH_STAGES.BUD/STEM/BLOOM` + `growthStartTime` from `millis()`); flowers restored from storage are forced to `BLOOM` with `isOld = true`.

**A flower GROWS, and the whole sequence is duplicated in both gardens.** `GROW_RISE` (3400ms) is the stem coming up out of nothing with a bud on its tip; `GROW_OPEN` (1700ms) is that bud opening into the bloom. `growEase` is a cubic ease-out, so it leaves the ground quickly and arrives slowly, which is the shape a plant actually grows in. `updateGrowthStages` reads the same two constants for its thresholds, so the stage a flower reports and the picture it draws can never disagree.

- **THE TIMINGS AND THE SHAPES ARE THE SAME IN BOTH GARDENS. Edit one and edit the other.** `GROW_RISE`, `GROW_OPEN`, `growEase`, `drawBudShape` and `drawFlowerWithGrowth` are character-for-character identical in `personal-garden-sketch.js` and `shared-garden-sketch.js`. A flower that took four seconds on one page and seven on the other would read as the two pages running at different speeds, which is the one thing a shared visual language cannot survive.
- **The bud is a real bud, not a small circle.** `drawBudShape` draws sepals, a teardrop body and a seam down it. A sphere on a stem reads as a pin, and the seam is what says the thing is closed and about to open rather than simply round.
- **The bloom comes OUT of the bud, it does not replace it.** Through `GROW_OPEN` the bloom scales 0.18 to 1 while the bud shrinks around it, so there is no frame where one picture swaps for another. A straight crossfade was the obvious alternative and it reads as two drawings dissolving rather than as one thing opening.
- **A BUD RIPENS THE SHORT WAY ROUND THE WHEEL, and `lerpColor` will not do it.** The skin goes from a green at hue 112 to the flower's own hue as `ripe` runs 0 to 1, and `lerpColor` moves every component in a straight line: green to a rose at 350 was therefore walked through 231, so the bud came out PURPLE on every warm flower and only looked right on the blues. `shortHue(from, to, t)` takes the shorter arc instead, sending it 112 to 51 to 350, down through yellow green and orange, which is how a bud actually colours up. Saturation and lightness stay straight lines; hue is the only one that is an angle. **Anything else in this project that interpolates a hue has the same trap waiting in it.**
- **`drawBudShape` sets `colorMode(HSL)` itself.** The gardens run in HSL, but a bud may be drawn from any call site, and a function that assumes the caller's colour mode paints grey the one time it is wrong.
- **THE LEAVES UNFOLD ON THE WAY UP, and they hold their height while the stem carries on past them.** Nothing drew them during growth at all: `drawFlowerStemAndLeaves` took over only once growth was over, so a pair of full sized leaves appeared in a single frame on a finished flower. They now come out at about one second and open over 900ms.
  - **Where they attach is a HEIGHT, not a parameter, and the two are different numbers.** `LEAF_T` is 0.60 along the stem's curve; as a fraction of the stem's height that is `bezierPoint(0, 0.4, 0.7, 1, 0.60)`, which is **0.6336**, confirmed against p5 at runtime. That figure does not depend on how long the stem is, because a rising stem is the finished one with every control point scaled by the same amount, so the 0.4 and 0.7 are constants.
  - **`stemParamAtFrac` bisects back to the parameter** that sits at a given fraction of the finished height, eighteen halvings, which is well inside a pixel. Drawn at a fixed parameter instead, the leaves slide up the stem with the tip, which no plant does; a real one puts out a leaf near the tip and then grows past it.
  - **`LEAF_AT` is the moment the tip first reaches that height**, `GROW_RISE * (1 - cbrt(1 - 0.6336))`, which is 967ms. `drawLeavesOnStem` takes an optional length multiplier and position so a finished flower calls it with neither and gets exactly what it always got.
  - **THEY FINISH WITH THE BLOOM, at 5100ms, not before it.** They first unfolded over 900ms and then sat at full size for the remaining three seconds, which made the flower the only thing still visibly growing and left the leaves reading as dropped in and stopped. `leafGrowth(elapsed)` runs from `LEAF_AT` to the end of the whole sequence, and the opening phase passes it too rather than drawing them full size.
  - **`leafEase` is a SMOOTHSTEP and it may not be `growEase`.** The stem's cubic ease-out is 0.93 done by the time the bloom starts opening, so stretching it over four seconds is the same early finish in a slower dress. A smoothstep is 0.632 there, verified at runtime, so a third of the growth is still to come while the petals open; it also leaves and arrives gently, which is what lets them start from nothing without reading as a pop.
- **NO DROP SHADOW UNTIL THE BLOOM IS NEARLY OPEN**, ramped in over the last third of `GROW_OPEN` through `drawBloom(f, shadowK)`. A bud has nothing to cast one, and switching the shadow on at the final frame would be the same pop somewhere else.
- **`bloomShadow` READS THE LIVE TRANSFORM, and that is a real bug fixed rather than a precaution.** The shape is drawn 6000 units off to the right and dragged back purely as its own shadow, and the offset and blur are DEVICE pixels the transform never touches. It used to convert them with the pixel density alone, which is right only while nothing else has scaled the canvas. The opening scales the bloom from 0.18 to 1, so an unaccounted scale of k left the shadow `6000 * (k - 1)` device pixels away: at 0.18 that is about 9800px off the left of the screen, and it slid in from there and snapped into place on the last frame. **That was the visible pop.** It takes the scale off `ctx.getTransform()` now, so a bloom drawn inside any `scale()` gets its shadow in the right place. Verified at three scales: the shadow's centre sits within half a pixel of the bloom's, and its width tracks the scale exactly.
- The old `drawBud` and `drawGrowingStem` are GONE from both files. Do not reintroduce them alongside this.

**THE LAST QUESTION IS THE ONE THE PROJECT IS NAMED AFTER, and it is its own screen.** The flow asked how the day felt, what shaped it, and whether there was anything else, and never once asked what anybody was glad of. It is `prompt1 -> prompt2 -> prompt3 -> prompt4 -> flowerPreview -> garden` now.

- **IT IS SKIPPABLE, AND THAT IS THE DESIGN RATHER THAN A CONCESSION.** Gratitude produced on demand to get past a screen is a toll, not gratitude, and this is the LAST card: three questions in and one tap from the flower is the cruellest place in the flow to put a wall. Requiring an answer was weighed again later and refused again for that reason.
- **THE SKIP IS NAMED RATHER THAN HIDDEN, which is the answer to the real worry.** Merely optional has its own fault: an optional box on the last card is the one a hurried visitor tabs past without ever reading, so the one question the project is named after becomes the one nobody answers. The button reads **"See Your Flower"** once there is text and **"See Your Flower anyway"** while the box is empty, so leaving it empty is a thing somebody notices doing rather than a default they fall through. `gladBtnLabel()` is the one place that decides, and it reads the FIELD rather than `gratitudeText`, so it cannot go stale; **both** of the places that clear the field call it, or the button keeps yesterday's label.
- **IT IS ONE WORD ADDED, NOT A DIFFERENT LABEL, on both counts.** The press does exactly the same thing either way, so a label that changed wholesale would read as a different control appearing where there is only one. And it may NOT promise planting: this button opens the flower PREVIEW and the planting is a tap further on, which is what ruled out "Plant without one". **"anyway" is the card's own word**, out of the line the rewrite shortened.
- **THE CARD IS THREE SHORT PIECES NOW, and each one carries a sentence the subtext used to.** The title is **"One small gratitude?"**, which has the project's word in it and carries "small is fine"; the line under it is **"A cup of tea counts. Write whatever you like."**, two short fragments on ONE line; and "an empty box is allowed" moved onto the button. **The second fragment is reassurance rather than instruction**, and it is the only thing on the card that says the subject is yours: "a cup of tea counts" answers how small is allowed and says nothing about how wide. Keep it to one line, which at this card's width is about 50 characters; the old three sentences wrapped to two and that was the complaint. It was a 23px heading over a three sentence paragraph that wrapped to two lines, which is a lot of reading for the quietest question in the flow. The placeholder went with it, from "Today I was glad of..." to "Today I was grateful for...", or the card asks for a gratitude and the box asks for something else.
- **A SCREEN OF ITS OWN RATHER THAN A SECOND BOX ON THE ONE BEFORE.** A card with two text areas on it is a form, and that card is already carrying the photo control. Being LAST is also what makes it the question you leave with.
- **`showStep` (~:3338) IS WHERE A STEP IS ADDED, not `updateScreen`**, which is dead and has been for a long time. Three edits: the flag, the wrap toggle, and the button wiring in the two neighbouring cards.
- **BOTH TEXTS FEED `chooseFlowerForMood`.** The keyword lists in `flowerMeanings` are full of gratitude words and this is the field most likely to hit them, so leaving it out would make the one question about gratitude the one question with no bearing on which flower grows. **Measured rather than assumed**: over all 56 combinations of rating and shaper, adding a gratitude sentence changes the species in **32** of them. The weighting is untouched, still two per keyword found anywhere in what was written.
- **`gratitude` IS ITS OWN FIELD ON THE FLOWER, not folded into `journal`.** The book prints the ask above the writing, and a day planted before this screen existed carries a journal and no gratitude, which has to stay tellable from a day that simply left it empty.
- **`.gj-glad` READS LOUDER THAN `.gj-quote` under it**, same left rule so the two are one block, in the garden's teal rather than the journal's grey green.
- **A NAME COLLISION COST A ROUND, and the console is what found it.** `buildUI` already had a `const card4`, the flower preview's card, so the new one threw `Identifier 'card4' has already been declared` and the whole sketch never ran: a blank garden with the page otherwise working. **Read the console rather than trusting that a script wrote what it meant to.**

**Accounts.** `personal-garden-auth.js` owns the Supabase client, every account screen and `GardenStore`, the single place the garden is read and written. The sketch knows nothing about accounts: `loadGarden` takes `window.__gardenBoot`, and `saveGarden`, `saveName`, `hasPlantedToday` and `markPlantedToday` all go through `GardenStore`. **Keep it that way.** Anything account-shaped, including the signed-in chip, is built by the auth module against `document.body`, not by `buildUI()`.

- **Four full screen steps, and only one may be on screen.** The sign in gate, the offer to carry a browser garden into a new account, and the set-a-new-password form all carry `.gg-overlay`, and each calls `clearOverlays()` before it builds. They used to share the id `gg-auth`, and the recovery form could appear on top of a gate that the session check had already raced ahead and built.
- **Everything appends through `onReady()`.** This module runs from the head, and whether `document.body` exists when the session check resolves is a network race. On a warm cache it resolved first and the sign in screen threw on `appendChild`, which locks everyone out.
- **The gate has a way past it without an account.** "Use this browser only, no account" drops straight into the localStorage mode the project has always had. Without it, configuring Supabase would make an account compulsory to use the garden at all.
- **A browser garden is offered, never taken.** On a first sign in where the account has no row (`freshAccount`) and this browser holds flowers, `offerLocal()` asks. `GardenStore.adoptLocal()` is the only thing that copies a local garden up, and it runs once, on that answer.
- **Password recovery is an auth state event, not a URL check alone.** `onAuthStateChange` fires `PASSWORD_RECOVERY` when the emailed link is opened; the `recovering` flag stops the garden booting behind the form. The reset needs the page's address listed under Supabase's Redirect URLs, which is step 5 of `SUPABASE-SETUP.md`.
- **`signOut` had no caller for its whole life.** A device could sign in with no way out or to switch account. `#gg-account` sits **bottom right**: Home is top left, and Save PNG and the music button are both top right. Bottom right is free only because of a bug, and the two are tied together. `buildLogo` creates `#gg-logo` inside `loadImage("LOGO-01.png", ...)`'s SUCCESS callback, and that file exists only at the repo root, so from `personal-garden/` it 404s and the callback never runs. **Fix that 404 and the logo reappears exactly under the chip**, at which point one of the two has to move.

**The sketch tag is not in the HTML.** `boot()` in the auth module injects `personal-garden-sketch.js` once there is a garden to draw, because p5 starts the moment it is parsed and its `preload()` cannot wait on a promise. Put the tag back in the HTML and the garden will render before the data arrives.

**An injected sketch has to be STARTED, and `boot()` does it by hand.** p5 in global mode only auto-starts from the window `load` event, and only if `window.setup` already exists at that moment. A sketch injected after a session check has resolved misses that event entirely, so the file parses, every function is defined, and nothing ever runs: a blank garden with no error in the console. `boot()` now calls `new p5()` from the tag's `onload`, guarded on `typeof window.frameCount === "undefined"` so it cannot start a second instance if p5 did manage to start one itself. `frameCount` is the tell, since p5 only defines it once a sketch is live. This had always been broken and was hidden by an unconfigured project, where the session check resolved synchronously and the sketch was injected before `load`.

**A STARTED SKETCH CAN STILL COME UP BLANK, and the second half of that is `windowWidth`.** The symptom is the same picture as the one above, which is what made it hard: the page's own flat background, the Home pill and the corner icons, nothing else, and **NO error in the console**. The difference is that p5 IS running; `frameCount` climbs. The canvas is 0 by 0.

- **p5 FILLS `windowWidth` AND `windowHeight` WHEN p5.js ITSELF IS PARSED, and refreshes them only on a resize event.** `setup()` called `createCanvas(windowWidth, windowHeight)`, so whatever the window reported while the HEAD was being parsed is what the canvas is sized to for ever. A tab that is in the background, restored from the back forward cache, or coming back on iOS reports **0** at that moment. `createCanvas(0, 0)` then makes a dead canvas and no resize ever follows to correct it.
- **The injection is what makes this likely rather than theoretical.** A sketch parsed with the page is created in the same breath as p5 itself; this one is created after a network round trip, so the gap between p5's cache being filled and the canvas being made is as long as a session check takes.
- **`viewW()` and `viewH()` are the fix, in BOTH sketches**, and they ask the window rather than p5: `window.innerWidth || document.documentElement.clientWidth || windowWidth || 0`. Every `createCanvas`, every `resizeCanvas`, `fitTitle`'s own 200px floor and the shared garden's two `isNarrow` tests go through them. **Nothing in either sketch may read `windowWidth` directly again.**
- **The shared garden had the identical lines and was fixed with it**, which is this project's standing rule: a bug found in one hand-ported sketch is a thing to go and look for in the other. It is less exposed there, since that sketch is a plain tag rather than an injection, but the fault is the same.
- **It was proved rather than guessed, and the proof is one line.** `window.dispatchEvent(new Event('resize'))` on the blank page brought `windowWidth` from 0 to 1024 and the whole garden appeared. After the fix, forcing `windowWidth = windowHeight = 0` and calling `windowResized()` leaves the canvas at full size where before it collapsed. **If a garden is ever blank again, read `width` and `height` before anything else**: p5 reporting 0 by 0 on a window that plainly is not says the canvas never got a size, and a blank page with no console error says almost nothing else.

**The gate, the carry-over offer and the reset form all stand IN the garden scene.** `.gg-overlay` paints the garden's own sky, sun and three hills as a CSS background, an inline SVG for the hills over a radial sun over the sky gradient, rather than a flat wash. The sun is `circle 190px`, a fixed radius and not a percentage: a percentage grows with the window and turns the sun into a wash across the whole sky. The card on top is the project's own `.gg-card` treatment, snow at radius 16 with the garden's shadow, a Fraunces 600 heading at 26px over a 15px line, and a button identical to `.gg-btn`. The heading is 26 and not `.gg-title`'s 42 for the reason given under Type: 42 belongs to a title standing on its own above a card, and this one is inside its card.

**`personal-garden-style.css` had no `box-sizing` rule anywhere**, and text inputs default to `content-box` while buttons default to `border-box`. The sign in fields therefore hung 26px over both edges of the card while the button under them fitted. `.gg-auth-card input, .gg-auth-card button { box-sizing: border-box }` is scoped to that card on purpose; a global `*` rule would re-lay out everything `buildUI()` sizes inline.

With `config.js` left on its placeholders, nothing is gated and the garden saves to this browser exactly as it always did, so the project still runs with no setup. `SUPABASE-SETUP.md` at the repo root holds the table, the row level security and the steps. The anon key is meant to be public; the RLS policies are what actually keep gardens apart.

`last_planted` lives on the account row, not in this browser, so the once a day limit follows someone between devices rather than resetting on each one.

Local keys, used only when no project is configured: `personal_gratitude_garden` (flower array JSON), `personal_gratitude_garden_name`, `gratitude_last_planted` (MM/DD/YYYY).

### Shared garden (`shared-garden/shared-garden-sketch.js`)

Same rendering engine, different state layer. `preload()` calls `partyConnect("wss://demoserver.p5party.org", "gratitude_garden")` and `partyLoadShared("garden-" + todayStr())`. **The room key is the date, so the garden resets daily, not weekly**, and the copy on the landing page and in the garden's own tips card both say so and everyone on that date shares one room. This points at the public p5.party demo server; there is no private backend.

- `shared.flowers` is the authoritative array. `draw()` diffs `flowers.length` against `shared.flowers.length` and re-copies on change, then re-attaches local-only animation fields (`growthStage`, `growthStartTime`, `isMyFlower`) by matching on `createdIndex` + `word`.
- `partyIsHost()` guards initialization and pruning in `initDailyGarden()`.
- Flow: `landing` → `username` → `select` → `confirm` → `garden`. Here the user picks the species directly from `speciesList`; there is no mood inference. Because the choice is the user's, `speciesList` carries a `meaning` for each species and the select tiles and the confirm card both show it. The wording is the personal garden's `flowerMeanings[...].meaning`, copied verbatim: the two gardens draw the same eight species, so a flower cannot stand for one thing on one page and something else on the other. **Change one and change the other.**
- `GARDEN_KEY` is vestigial, since this page uses no `localStorage`.
- (Resolved) `personal-garden-index.html` carried an inline `<style>` in its head holding `#save-btn { top: 16px !important; }`. That one line beat both the stylesheet and the sketch's own inline `.style("top", ...)`, so the Save PNG button stayed where it was however the JS was edited, and reading `saveBtn.style.top` back reported the value the JS had asked for rather than where the button actually was. The override is gone; the sketch's inline value already beat the stylesheet on its own. **Check where an element renders, not what its style attribute says.**
- (Resolved) The colour step washed the whole viewport with `rgba(207, 238, 240, 0.85)` on `#confirm-wrap`, so the garden appeared to vanish the moment you reached it. Every other step leaves its `.gg-wrap` transparent and lets the card stand on the scene. If a step ever loses the garden again, look for a background on its wrap.
- (Resolved) `showStep` never made `#garden-wrap` visible, so the CSS `display: none` on it stood at every step and the sky, hills and ground never appeared behind anything. The personal garden shows its wrap unconditionally for exactly this reason, and the shared one now does too. If a garden ever renders on a blank background again, this is the line to check.

#### Who a flower is from, and looking before planting

**THREE WAYS TO BE ON A FLOWER, CHOSEN ON THE CONFIRM STEP.** `identity` holds `"gardener"`, `"name"` or `"anon"`.

| | what the garden shows |
|---|---|
| `gardener` | the figure stands beside the flower, with the name on it |
| `name` | the name alone, which is what this garden always did, and the DEFAULT |
| `anon` | the label reads Someone, and nothing identifying is stored |

- **THE IDENTITY QUESTION IS A SECTION, NOT A THIRD PICKER.** It sits AFTER the colour, which is the right order: the two flower choices stay together and the privacy line is the last thing read before the button that acts on it. What was wrong was the WEIGHT. `idLabel` carried the colour label's exact size and colour, so a question about whether your name goes on a public flower read as a peer of "Choose your flower color:". A hairline rule above it marks where the card stops being about the bloom and starts being about you, and the label is an uppercase eyebrow at 12.5px, which is this page's own language for a section beginning. **Moving it above the colour was refused**: it would put a privacy decision between somebody and the flower they came to make.
- **NAME IS THE DEFAULT ON PURPOSE.** Making anonymous the default would empty the meadow of people to buy a setting most visitors never open, and a shared garden of unnamed flowers is a private garden with strangers' flowers in it. Somebody who touches none of this gets exactly the garden they got before.
- **ANONYMOUS IS HONEST OR IT IS NOTHING.** The room object is PUBLIC and anybody can read it, so storing the name and hiding it at the draw would be a lie told somewhere people can check. `word` is left EMPTY and no look is written.
- **THE COST IS REAL AND IS ACCEPTED.** `sharedPlantedToday` reads names out of the room, so it cannot see an anonymous flower and only the browser marker then holds that person to one a day. That is the same guarantee this garden has always given anyone willing to clear their storage; planting here takes no account on purpose.
- **`getMyFlower` ASKS `isMyFlower` FIRST NOW.** It matched on the name, and every anonymous flower's name is the same empty string, so it would have handed back somebody else's.
- **`flowerLabel(f)` IS THE ONE PLACE A BLOOM'S LABEL IS DECIDED**, because it is read by the draw and again by `keepFlowersOnScreen`, which holds a flower in far enough for its own label. The two disagreeing would push blooms off the screen edge.
- **THE GARDENER OPTION ONLY APPEARS IF THERE IS ONE**, and if the gardener is removed while the card is open the choice falls back to `name` rather than leaving a dead selection. `GardenGardener.onChange` rebuilds the row.

**`drawGardener` BECAME `drawGardeners`, AND THE LOOK TRAVELS WITH THE FLOWER.** That is the whole point: the old one drew a single figure beside the one flower marked `isMyFlower`, because the room held nothing about anybody else, so a shared garden was a place where only you had a face.

- **A FIGURE IS DRAWN ONLY FOR A FLOWER WHOSE OWN RECORD CARRIES A LOOK**, never guessed, so a bloom planted as a name or anonymously has nobody at it, which is exactly what those choices mean.
- **`MAX_GARDENERS` IS 6, and twenty in one meadow is a lobby.** That is the conclusion the 3D exploration reached about the same question and for the same reason: what is left of somebody who was here for a minute is their flower, not a picture of them. The NEWEST are kept, then re-sorted into planting order so a nearer figure never paints behind a further one.
- **ONLY YOUR OWN TAKES THE PAT ANIMATION**, since the pat clock is one for the whole page and feeding it to every figure would wink the entire meadow at once. **Any of them can be patted**, though: a garden where only your figure answers has one real person in it and five cardboard ones.
- Verified with nine flowers carrying looks: six figures drawn, all on screen, and an anonymous flower draws none and labels Someone.

**LOOKING ROUND BEFORE PLANTING, as a FLAG rather than a step.** The garden was reachable only by planting in it, which asks somebody to write the one sentence they get for the day before they have seen the place or what anybody else wrote. `browsing` routes the landing step straight to the garden.

- **THE NUDGE IS A LINE, NOT A GATE.** Nothing blocks, nothing counts down and nothing closes. It rides in `#daily-note`, the box that already sits level with the corner icons, and carries the way back to writing. That is the only version of this that belongs on a page about not being hurried.
- **`#daily-note` IS `pointer-events: none`** so it never swallows a tap meant for a flower under it, so the one control inside it has to turn them back on for itself.
- Whatever was typed stays in the field, so taking the look costs nothing.

**A WARNING FROM TESTING THIS: THE ROOM IS LIVE AND PUBLIC, AND IT IS EASY TO WRITE TO BY ACCIDENT.** While checking the new drawing against the real room, `look` and `anon` ended up written onto a flower somebody had actually planted that day. It was found by reading the room back, and removed. A later probe confirmed that writing to a local `flowers` entry does NOT reach `shared.flowers`, so the two are genuine copies and the write came in by another route; the cause was not pinned down. **Test this page against a scratch array or a local room, read `shared.flowers` back afterwards, and never take it on trust that a local edit stayed local.**

### Shared garden: likes and comments (`shared-garden-social.js`)

**The shared garden stays OPEN.** Planting is still a typed name and a sentence, with no account and no Supabase call of any kind. What needs an account is REACTING: a like has to be countable once per person, and a comment has to carry a name that belongs to somebody. A gate on the page was the other option and it was refused on purpose, since the shared garden is the one place anyone can try in ten seconds and asking for an account at the door would cost that to buy a feature most visitors never touch.

**Where a flower lives is split, and the split is the whole design:**

| | holds | resets |
|---|---|---|
| p5.party | the living garden, the authority on what is planted today | daily, with the room key |
| Supabase | one row per flower somebody has reacted to, plus its likes and comments | never |

- **The Supabase row is created LAZILY**, the first time a signed in person opens that bloom, not when it is planted. So an anonymous planter costs the database nothing, there is no anonymous insert path to abuse, and the open garden stays open. A bloom and its row are tied together by `day` plus `created_index`, which p5.party already assigns and never reuses within a day.
- **A unique index on `(day, created_index)` is load bearing.** Two people opening the same bloom in the same second would each create a row and the comments would split across the two. Losing that race is normal rather than an error: the insert fails, and the code reads back the row the other person just made.
- **`likes` carries a unique index on `(post_id, user_id)`.** One like per person is enforced in the DATABASE, not in the page, because anyone can edit the page in their own browser.
- **The sketch knows almost nothing about this.** `handleGardenPointer` calls `GardenSocial.open(f, ctx)` on a tap and `GardenSocial.close()` on a tap into empty ground. That is the entire contract.
- **`roomKey` and `speciesList` are handed over at the call site**, not read off `window`. The sketch declares them with `let` and `const`, and neither lands on `window` in a classic script. Reaching for them was the first attempt and it filed every flower under `garden-unknown`, which is one bucket for every day at once.
- **The hover tooltip is untouched.** It answers a different question: the tooltip is what the flower SAYS at a glance, the panel is what other people have said back.
- **Commenter names come from a second query, not an embedded join.** A PostgREST join would need a foreign key from `comments` straight to `profiles`, and this schema points at `auth.users`, so asking for one would be a guess about somebody else's database. The comments are fetched, then every distinct commenter in one `profiles?id=in.(...)`.
- **A profile row is created on first sign in if it is missing**, since nothing else in the project creates one and a comment with no name under it is worse than no comment. `username` must be unique, so a collision retries once with the account's own id appended, which cannot collide.
- **Supabase answers a query against a missing column with a 400 and an error OBJECT, not a thrown exception.** A plain `res.data` read therefore turns "you have not run the SQL yet" into "this flower has no comments", which is the single most likely thing to go wrong on setup. `noteError` names it once in the console and the panel says so in words, pointing at Step 6 of `SUPABASE-SETUP.md`.
- **That message once fired on a database that was PERFECTLY SET UP, and the fix is worth keeping in mind for anything that blames the user's configuration.** Three things were wrong at once. The test was `/column|schema cache|does not exist|relation/`, and "relation" and "column" appear in Postgres errors that have nothing to do with a missing table, a permission refusal among them. The flag was STICKY for the life of the page, so one bad moment at any point made every flower opened afterwards carry the same wrong explanation. And the panel never showed what Supabase had actually said, so the wrong guess was the only thing anybody could read. Now the test is only `schema cache` or `does not exist`, `clearErrors()` runs on every panel open, and the real message is kept in `lastError` and shown. **A message that tells somebody to go and run SQL has to be right, because they will go and run it.**
- **Verified against the live project with the publishable key**: `posts`, `likes` and `comments` all answer, every column Step 6 creates is present, and the exact query the panel runs returns cleanly. An RLS refusal comes back as `new row violates row-level security policy for table "posts"`, which is correctly NOT a schema problem.
- **Signing up with confirmation emails left on returns a user and NO session.** The panel says to check the email rather than looking signed in while every write is refused.
- **The whole feature is optional.** With no project configured, or with the Supabase library missing, `GardenSocial.open` does nothing and the garden behaves exactly as it did before any of this existed.
