- **It is a BUTTON, not a field.** An input would promise that typing in it is the thing that signs you up, and the real form has two fields and lives in `garden-account.js`.
- **It opens `GardenAccount.openSignIn()`**, which is what the chip in the corner already opens, so there is one sign in on the page rather than two. Signed in it opens the friends panel instead, and with accounts not configured it says so on the button rather than opening nothing.
- **Instagram is `gratitudegarden.id`**, beside the existing Linktree, as inline SVG rather than an image file: this repo ships three images in total and draws everything else in code.
- **`.footer-col ul` ALREADY SETS `flex-direction: column`** and outranks a bare class, so the social row has to be scoped `.footer-col ul.footer-social` or the two icons stack.
- Explore gained Build your gardener, so all four pages are named there.

**THE FOOTER IS TWO COLUMNS, NOT TWO EDGES.** `.footer-top` was `justify-content: space-between` on a 939px row carrying 403px of content, which left a **535px gulf** between Gratitude Garden and Explore, and the links were `align-items: flex-end` so they were ragged down their left side against the page edge. It is an even two column grid with a 3rem gap now, both columns left aligned, and the brand description can run to 340px rather than 260 since it has half the row. Measured after: the gap between the columns is 51px.

- **The vertical pair is even, and it had to be measured rather than set.** The gap above the divider is the 2.5rem margin under `.footer-top`, which reads 43px. `.footer-bottom` centres its credit in whatever is left after its padding, which adds about 12px on top, so 1.5rem read 38 and 2.5rem read 55. At **1.8rem** the gap under the divider reads 43 and matches the one above it exactly.
- **The phone block stacks a GRID now.** It set `flex-direction: column`, which does nothing to a grid, so it also needs `grid-template-columns: 1fr` or the two columns stay side by side on a phone.

**Every curved section clears its curve by the same amount: 100px on desktop, 56px on mobile.** The curve is `top: -60px; height: 100px`, so it dips 40px INTO the section below it, and the gap between that and the first line of copy is what reads as headroom. It was 20px in the footer against 124 in the closing section. **The top padding is not the clearance**: `.about`, `.gardens`, `.catalogue` and `.cta` each carry 24px between their padding and their first painted element and `.wisdom` and the footer carry none, which is why 116 and 140 both land on 100. Measure the first painted child, do not read the padding, and force `.reveal` elements `visible` first or its 24px transform is counted as spacing.
  - **The footer is set three times and the later rule wins every time.** Its base rule, the 1100px block and the 768px block all set `padding`, and the two blocks were overriding the headroom outright. This is the same trap as `.cta` being set twice in the mobile block.
  - **The cost was paid by `.gardens` and `.catalogue`,** which went from 1.23 and 1.04 screens to 1.26 and 1.08. `.about` and `.cta` were the two that LOST height, and they were already the shortest, so the padding their tops gave up was added to their bottoms and both hold their tuned 0.84 and 0.74 exactly.
- **A curve is only visible where two colours meet,** so the colours decide where the curves can go.
- **Every curve points DOWN, and there are no exceptions left.** A section curves into the one below by carrying a `::before` in the colour above, never a `::after` in the colour below. The tips section used to be the only one with a curve on both edges and the only one curving upward; the closing section carries that join on its own top now, and the footer carries the last one.
- **About's curve is a second curve, and that is deliberate but unsettled.** The meadow's ground already has a curved top edge from the hills, so `.about::before` puts another curve a short way below it. It was left out twice for exactly that reason and is now in on purpose, to see whether it reads as layered ground or as a mistake. **If it is the second, delete `.about::before` and the `position`/`overflow` on `.about`; that is the whole change.**
- **The flower guide's eight tiles are white with a `1.5px solid var(--sky)` border**, so that section must never take a sky ground or the border vanishes. It is cream. The cards do not need a border to read, because every one carries `box-shadow: 0 8px 32px rgba(29,100,102,0.10)` which draws its edge whatever the ground.
- **The PALE card is `#8fcfbe`, the meadow's own teal, and the personal garden holds it now.** The personal and bouquet cards swapped: the personal garden is the pale teal, the bouquet carries the dark green gradient, and every ink, badge, small print note, hint, fold button and chevron swapped with them, including the two inline note icons in `index.html`, which were drawn one for a dark ground and one for a pale one. `--bouquet-ink` is renamed **`--pale-card-ink`**, since it belongs to whichever card is pale rather than to the bouquet. All fifteen text elements were re-measured against the gradient colour actually behind each one, not against the card's worst stop: the lowest is the bouquet's small print at 5.29. **Measure a card's text against the gradient AT THAT ELEMENT'S POSITION.** Checking everything against the lightest stop reported two false failures, because the badge and the small print sit at the top of the card where the gradient is at its darkest. The teal was originally chosen for the bouquet because: the front strip of the hero's ground. It ties the card back to the top of the page and, more to the point, it is the only candidate that moved the HUE:

  | candidate | vs its ground | vs the yellow card beside it |
  |---|---|---|
  | `--sky` | L1.23 **dH4** | L1.19 |
  | `#8fcfbe` | L1.59 **dH16** | L1.53 |

  **Luminance alone is not enough when two colours share a hue.** Sky measured better than every pale alternative and still read as the same colour as the section behind it, because four degrees of hue is nothing. This is the lesson the whole colour pass kept teaching: check hue distance alongside contrast, and check a card against both of its neighbours, its ground and the card beside it.
- **Anything written on the bouquet card uses `--bouquet-ink`, `#14494b`.** Plain `--darkteal` on that teal measures 3.86, under the 4.5 body text needs; this is 5.68. The title, the description, the small print, the hint and the badge all take it, and so does the fold chevron on a phone.
- **Section heights are tuned, not accidental.** Nothing on the page should run much past one screen or fall much under three quarters of one. About was 0.74 wedged between a 1.00 hero and a 1.23 cards section; merging it into a single cream run with the cards instead produced a 1858px block, two full screens against neighbours of about one, which was worse. Splitting them and padding the ends is what balances it: 1.00, 0.84, 1.23, 1.04, 0.80, 0.74.
- **Three sections carry extra padding for that, and all three are scoped to desktop.** `.about` 130px, `.wisdom` and `.cta` 140px, against the standard 85px. Stacked into one column the copy wraps and they are already in proportion, so the mobile block sets 72px and 56px. **`.cta` is set twice in that block** and the later rule wins, so change both.
- **About is padded 130px rather than the standard 85px,** and 72px rather than 37px on a phone. At the standard padding it was 0.74 of a screen wedged between a 1.00 hero and a 1.23 cards section and read as a strip rather than a stop. It is 0.84 now, and running it into the cards as one cream region of about 1860px is the other half of that fix. The override is scoped: stacked into one column its copy wraps and it is already the tallest section, so the desktop value there would pad out something that needs no help.
- **The hero ends on a straight horizon, and that is deliberate.** Two other things were tried there and both were wrong for the same reason: **the ground already has a curved top edge from the hills**, so fading the grass out, or giving About a curve of its own, put a second soft edge a few pixels under the first. The meadow now stops on a clean line and the next curve is a whole section lower down.
- **A section with `overflow: hidden` does not clip the flower modal,** which is `position: fixed` and has no transformed ancestor. Adding a transform or a filter to a section would break that.

Section order is `home, about, gardens, flowers, wisdom, start`. There was a `how` section between about and gardens, one line per experience; it went because the three cards under it already show what it described, in the same order, with pictures. Adding or moving a section means four edits, not one: the markup, the `.side-nav-item` order and its `data-section`, the `sectionIds` array that drives the scroll spy, and the styles. All four must agree or the nav highlights the wrong thing. The hero's second button points at `#flowers` for the same reason: it used to point at `#how`.

**The three card buttons are one fill, and the dark card adds a ring.** All three are dark teal with a cream label. That measures 4.06 against the pale teal card and 6.04 against the yellow one, but **1.24 against the dark green one, and no single fill can fix that**: clearing 3:1 against the yellow card needs a luminance under 0.26 and clearing it against the dark green card needs over 0.52, so no colour does both. `.btn-on-dark` keeps the same fill and adds a cream inset ring, 4.29 against that card, and there it is the ring that draws the shape. `.btn-card-light` is still in the stylesheet; nothing uses it now.

**The bouquet has a panel too, and it is CSS rather than canvas.** `#bouquetShow::before` fills the builder's own backdrop form, `linear-gradient(160deg, ...)` from `bouquet-sketch.js:175`, with the garden's sky rather than one of the eight presets, so the row reads as three framed pictures. No hills and no ground, because a cut bouquet is not planted in a meadow. **It has to be a `::before` on the slot and not a fill on the canvas:** the letter tag tucks under the wrap at `z-index` 1 with the canvas at 2, so an opaque canvas would swallow it, and at `z-index` 0 the panel sits behind both. It stops 22px short of the bottom, where the garden canvases stop, since that strip belongs to the hint line.

**A badge's ICON has to swap with its card too.** The card swap moved every background, border and text colour but left the two inline SVGs alone, so REFLECT kept a white icon after its card went pale and GIVE kept a dark teal one after its card went dark. GIVE's simply was not there. Icons are inline `stroke` and `fill` attributes in `index.html`, not CSS, which is why a stylesheet-wide swap misses them.

**A card's badge and its small print share one tint.** The bouquet card's note carried a yellow wash while the badge directly above it carried a white one, so two chips on the same card were different colours, and both of the other cards already matched. The note takes `.badge-bouquet`'s values exactly, and its icon follows its text to cream. **The shapes stay different on purpose**: the badge is a 50px pill and the note a 12px rounded rectangle, on all three cards, because the badge is a label and the note is a sentence.

**The pale cards' badges and small print are a tint, never a white pill.** The personal card's were `rgba(255,255,255,0.62)` and `0.58` while the other two carried an 0.08 to 0.15 wash, so it read as noticeably heavier than the row it sits in. It takes the yellow card's treatment, which is the other pale card.

**One filled button per group, the rest outline.** The page had no `.btn-primary` on it at all: the hero carried two outline buttons and the closing section three, so the two most important actions on the page were also the quietest things on it. `Personal Garden` in the closing section is solid; everything beside it stays outline.

**The sky belongs to the hero, the sun belongs to the gardens, and both now run everywhere.** The page carried two clouds and two suns: the hero drew a rounded bar with two circles sitting on it, the gardens drew a symmetric trio, the hero's sun was a plain disc that pulsed and the gardens' was a soft glow with twelve rays. They were swapped, so the whole project draws one of each.
  - **The cloud lives in four places now**, and they all use the hero's fractions of the bar's width W: the bar is W by 0.269W with its top 0.19W down, bump one is 0.433W at 0.138W from the left flush with the top, bump two is 0.314W at 0.456W and 0.052W down, at alpha 0.9. Those are `drawClouds()` in both garden sketches and `cloud()` in `index.html`'s card panels, plus the hero's own CSS which is where the numbers came from. `c.size` is still the old centre circle diameter so stored clouds are untouched; W is 1.6 of it, the width the old trio spanned.
  - **The hero's sun is inline SVG, not a gradient**, because the rays have to be the shape `drawSunGradient()` draws: eight circles from r60 inward at rising opacity, then twelve rays from r55 to r90. **It is positioned off its DISC, not its box.** The svg is 180 units wide but only 120 of that is disc, so at 132px the disc is the 88px the old circle was, and `top` and `right` are pulled back by the 22px of ray that now sits outside it. It does not pulse, because the gardens' does not.
  - **p5's `draw()` is rAF driven and the browser pane is hidden here**, so a garden loads with `frameCount` at 0 and an empty canvas. Call `redraw()` a few times before sampling pixels, or you will measure a blank sky and think the clouds are broken.

**The bouquet is inset from its panel at BOTH ends.** `availH` takes 22 for the hint strip and then 8 twice, top and bottom. With only the top 8 the painted bottom landed at 278, exactly where the panel's 2px ring runs, and the wrap covered 104 of the 552 device pixels across it, so the frame read as broken open at the foot. The ring is on the `::before` at `z-index` 0 and the canvas is at 2, so the canvas always wins there; the fix is to keep the bouquet off it.

**THE LANDING PAGE'S PERSONAL CARD TOOLTIP IS NOW THE OLD GARDEN ONE, and the two have deliberately diverged.** The card copied the garden's five row box to the pixel, `Mood:`, `Shaped by:`, `Entry:`, a rule and the date. The garden's own box has since been cut to a glance, because tapping a flower there opens the book. The card is a still picture of a garden with nothing to tap, so the long box is still the more useful thing to show and it was left alone. **If it is ever brought back into line, the tooltip gets SHORTER, which only loosens the `headY` arithmetic below rather than breaking it.**

**One of the two personal card blooms carries an `Entry:` line and the other does not.** That is what says the journal field exists and is optional, which is also how the garden behaves: it shows the line only when something was written. It costs 15px of tooltip, 93 to 108, and the rose still clears its own head at `headY` 166 with 20px to spare.

**Opacity on text is the same bug as a translucent colour, and the audit has to read it.** `.section-desc` was caught years-equivalent ago; three more were not, because a contrast check that reads `color` and composites it over the ground **silently ignores `opacity`** and reports the solid number. Measured properly: `.cs-hint` was 2.76, 2.75 and 3.11 at 11.56px, `.hero-subtitle` 3.50 at 17.85px, `.flower-modal-quote` 4.31 at 16.15px. They are 5.06/4.55/4.52, 5.78 and 4.94 now. `.cs-hint` sits at 0.87, the point all three cards clear 4.5; the hero subtitle went solid at `#2f6260`, the body copy floor; the modal quote at 0.86. **Include `opacity` in any contrast check on this page**, and check the rendered number rather than the declared colour.

**The hero text is centred by auto margins, not by a padding.** `.meadow-scene` already carries `margin-top: auto`, so a second auto margin on `.hero-text-block` splits the free space evenly and the block lands halfway between the top of the page and the flowers at any window height. With the old fixed `padding-top: 140px` it sat 160 from the top and 316 above the meadow, which was obvious the moment the button under it went. **Measure the balance against the meadow's first PAINTED pixel, not its canvas top:** the canvas is transparent sky for its top 28px, so measuring to its box reports 206 against 166 where the eye sees 206 against 194. Two margins that only existed to separate the copy from the button went with it, the 2.5rem under the block and the 1.8rem under the subtitle; left in, the second was 30px of dead space inside the block and the autos centred the box rather than the words. Mobile puts the 2.5rem back, since `height: auto` there leaves no free space for the autos to divide.

**A gradient reads by its HUE drift, not its luminance range.** The personal card's three stops all sat at roughly hue 163, and it looked like one flat colour despite a 0.084 luminance range that is **wider** than the yellow card's 0.062. The yellow card only reads as a gradient because it swings 51 degrees to pale green at the end. The personal card's last stop is now 52 degrees away, `#c9e6c4`, for a 0.188 range; its top half stays teal, so it is still the teal card.

**The three panels are matched by weight, and the bouquet's is the one that has to be tuned.** Run to `#f9ffff` its panel averaged 0.898 luminance against the two garden panels' 0.72, and on the dark card it separated at 6.09 against their 1.18 and 1.32, so the third card pulled the whole row. Ending on `#a9d9cf`, the garden's back hill tone, puts it at 0.716 and 4.93. It cannot match their separation exactly, because that is the dark card's doing, not the panel's.

**The hint lines say "Tap or hover".** Under 768px the cards fold and the blooms are tap-to-open, which the pointer handler has always supported, so "Hover" alone was wrong on every phone.

**The hero carries no button at all now.** It had two, then one, and the last one had the same problem as the two before it: it only scrolled further down the same page, which a scroll gesture and the side nav already do. Pointing it at About rather than the gardens would have made it scroll to the immediately adjacent section, the emptiest version of the same action, so it went instead. `.hero-buttons` and its two mobile rules went with it. The page's one filled button is the closing section's.

**The closing section's buttons wrap, never nowrap.** Three `nowrap` buttons measured wider than `.cta-inner` and ran straight out through both sides of it. The box is 820px and `.cta-buttons` wraps, so a longer label can only ever push a button onto a second row.

**The bouquet is called "The Bouquet Builder"**, parallel to "The Personal Garden" and "The Shared Garden". Any new copy uses that name; "Build a Bouquet" is gone from every page including the builder's own `<title>`.

**The bouquet has no section of its own.** It is the third card in `#gardens`, alongside the two garden cards, because a section of its own carried five numbered steps that simply restated the How it works column below it. Anything that introduces the bouquet belongs in that card or in `.how-col-bouquet`, not in a fourth place.

**NEITHER DOES THE GARDENER. It is a BAND at the foot of `#gardens`**, under the three cards and sharing their pale blue, and that is the colours deciding rather than taste. It had a section of its own between the flower guide and Wisdom, which was the ONLY gap a new section fits without recolouring: every section is one flat colour and every join is a curve painted in the colour ABOVE, and **the flower guide can never take pale blue**, because its eight white tiles are bordered in `--sky` and the border vanishes. A band needs no curve, no colour, no side-nav entry and no sparkle canvas, it sits far higher up the page, and it reads in the right order: here are the three places, and here is who stands in them.

- **IT MUST BE A SIBLING OF `.gardens-grid`, NOT A CHILD.** Put inside the grid it becomes a FOURTH CELL, which is the fourth-card outcome the band exists to avoid; measured that way it also left 294px of empty ground under the button. As a direct child of the section it ends 59px above the curve, which is the section's own bottom padding.
- **THE BUTTON UNDER THE ROW GOES TO THE CHOICES, WHICH IS WHAT IT SAYS.** It read "Or build one from nothing" and pointed at the bare build page, which opens the EDITOR for anybody who has already made a gardener: a button inviting you to start showed you the middle of something you were half way through. It says "Start customising" and carries `?start=1`, which shows the gallery WITHOUT clearing anything, so walking back out leaves the saved gardener as it was.
- **THE FIVE EXAMPLES ARE LINKS, and each carries `?t=`**, so pressing one opens the build page already wearing that gardener. They looked pressable and were not, and the answer was to make them the templates rather than to make them look inert. No card panel behind them: five white cards in a row directly under three white garden cards reads as a second set of cards.
- **They read `AvatarDraw.TEMPLATES`**, which is the same list the build page's gallery reads, so the row here and the gallery there cannot drift.
- **They bob, each on its own period**, or the five rise and fall together and the row reads as the page tilting. Same rule the hero's objects follow, and it is off under `prefers-reduced-motion`.
- **Three and two on a phone, not five.** Five across 375 gives each gardener about 62px of column, at which the flower on their head is ten pixels and the row reads as five smudges.

**The bouquet card's illustration is drawn by the bouquet's own renderer.** `index.html` loads `bouquet/bouquet-sketch.js` and calls `renderBouquetCanvas` on `#landingBouquet`, rather than carrying a ninth copy of the flower code that would drift the moment the bouquet changed. Two things make that safe, and both must stay true: `boot()` returns immediately when `#bqBuilder` is absent, so none of the builder runs on the home page; and every one of the landing page's own `drawTulip` style functions lives inside an IIFE, so it does not collide with the sketch's top level names. If either ever stops holding, the home page breaks loudly.

**The cards carry no bullet list.** Each had three perks under its paragraph, which said the same thing the paragraph above them had just said, in shorter words, and pushed the button off the bottom of the screen. Removing them took about 80px off every card. A card is now badge, title, paragraph, the small print line, the picture and the button.

**A RESIZE IS NOT A REASON TO FOLD ANYTHING, and on a phone it fires constantly.** `applyFolding` shuts every card, which is right when the layout crosses 768 and wrong at every other moment, and it was reached from the window resize handler by way of `initFolding`. iOS collapses its address bar as you scroll, which fires a RESIZE, so opening a card and then scrolling closed it again under your thumb. `applyFolding` now remembers which side of the breakpoint it last folded for and does nothing unless that changed, and the page's resize handler ignores anything that is not a WIDTH change, which also stops eleven canvases repainting for a scroll.

**The three cards fold on small screens.** Stacked in one column they ran to nearly three screens of scrolling, so under 768px each collapses to its badge and title behind a chevron and only one opens at a time. All three start closed. Two things are load bearing: `.gardens-grid` must switch to `grid-auto-rows: auto` there, since the `1fr` that keeps the row of three level on a wide screen makes every row as tall as the tallest in one column, so opening one card stretched the closed ones to match and the fold saved nothing; and the slot's `_paint` must run when a card opens, because a canvas inside a `display: none` box measures zero and comes back blank. Folded 151px each, 855px for the section; one open, 1500px, against about 2600px before.

**TOUCHING A HERO FLOWER THROWS THE GARDENS' OWN SPARKLE.** Nine four pointed stars, the geometry `drawNewestSparkles` uses, out and fading over 900ms, tinted by that flower's hue.

- **The hit test reads positions the DRAW recorded, not the data.** `allFlowers` and every `drawDaisy` style function are declared INSIDE `draw()`, so nothing outside that function can see them; a first attempt at a hit test against `allFlowers` threw a `ReferenceError` on every tap, silently, inside a pointer handler. `draw()` now pushes each bloom's landed position into a `hits` array as it paints it. That is better than the data anyway: it carries the sway, and it holds only the flowers that survived `minSpacing`, so what is tappable is exactly what is on the screen.
- **The pointer is converted through the canvas's measured width**, not by assuming its box matches its CSS width. In an embedded or scaled view the two differ, and the tap then lands nowhere near the flower under the finger.
- **The tick loop no longer returns early under `prefers-reduced-motion`.** Reduced motion opts out of the drifting sway, not out of an answer to a finger somebody deliberately put on a flower, so the loop runs when there is a burst alive and the sway stays still.
- The canvas needs `pointer-events: auto` set in JS, since a tap has to reach it at all.

**The hero flowers sway.** Both gardens sway, so a still hero was the odd one out. It is deliberately small: `SWAY` is 3.2px at the bloom and nothing at the base, since the stem's control points take a fraction of the same offset, and each flower is offset in the cycle by its own x or the whole row leans together and reads as the page tilting. It runs at about 30 frames rather than 60, only while the hero is on screen, and not at all under `prefers-reduced-motion`. The geometry is measured in `measure()` and cached: reading the element's box per frame was 30 forced layouts a second for a number that only changes on resize.

**Where a species repeats in the hero, the repeats are pulled well apart.** Three sunflowers at 44, 18 and 62 and two daisies at 15 and 52. They were 44, 38, 50 and 15, 15, and at a few degrees apart a repeat reads as the same flower drawn twice rather than two people's choices.

**All three cards share one picture slot.** `.card-art-slot` is a fixed 250px box under the copy and above the button, holding the two gardens and the bouquet alike. It is positioned, never flexed: a canvas carries an intrinsic size and a flex item may shrink below its stated width, so a canvas laid out by flex collapses to its intrinsic width.

The meadows were once full-bleed backdrops behind the copy under a gradient, and the bouquet could not join them there: a bouquet is tall and its paper and ribbon sit at the bottom, exactly where such a gradient is at its most opaque, so the card erased the most recognisable part of its own picture. A lavender spike paints well over twice the height it is given and grows upward from its origin, so `paintBloom` gives it a smaller head and a shorter stem than the round blooms; the meadow scenes that used to fill these slots hit the same thing and had to be retuned for it.

**All three cards hold one still picture,** the thing you end up with, and nothing moves. They ran a three slide loop for a while, stepping through each flow on a 3.6 second timer, and it was both too busy to read and too small to be accurate: every screen had to be shrunk to about half size to fit, and half size is where the real questions, dates and usernames stop being legible. One picture at full garden size says more. `CARD_SHOWS` in `index.html` holds the two gardens' flowers; the bouquet card's block further down draws its own, after `bouquet-sketch.js` has loaded.

- **A flower's position is `headY`, how far its bloom sits from the TOP of the frame, not the length of its stem.** Measured from the ground instead, making the frame taller pushed every head down with it and the tooltips stopped landing at `TOP_Y`. The stem is derived: `floorY - headY`.
- **The flowers are the size the garden draws them, not a size that fits.** Both gardens give their first ten flowers a radius of 28 to 36 and print white text over a halo of the flower's own colour: the personal garden prints **the whole date**, `08/30/2026`, at 13px with four offsets, and the shared garden the username at 16px with eight, cut at 11 characters. Neither size varies by species. This is why the personal card holds **two** flowers and the shared card three: the date is wider than the flower it is printed on, in the garden as much as here, so two dates have to be pushed to opposite sides of the frame and a third will not fit. At 375px the canvas is 219 wide and the two dates measure 32 to 96 and 123 to 187, which is the whole of the margin there is. Three usernames fit because a username is short, and three is also what says the garden is shared.
- **The reveal's flower tooltip is snow, like the gardens'.** `.bq-bloom-tip` was dark teal with cream text, which read as a different product's tooltip sitting on the same page. The gardens pop a near white box with dark text when you hover a flower, and it does now too.
- **The tooltip is placed by the gardens' own arithmetic:** `top = flowerY - radius * 1.5 - boxHeight - 5`, then pulled back inside the frame, which is why a flower near an edge gets a box that is no longer centred on it. That is the garden's behaviour, not a bug. The one change is the top of that clamp: it is `TOP_Y = 8`, which is where the bouquet canvas starts in the card alongside, so a message that runs off the top lands level with the bouquet instead of at an arbitrary 4. Each flower's `stem` is then chosen so its box does clamp there *and* still clears its own head, which is why they differ: the personal garden's box is five rows and the shared garden's is one. Its contents match too: the personal garden stacks the species and meaning in bold, then `Mood:`, `Shaped by:`, `Entry:`, a rule and the date in grey, at 12px with 15px lines; the shared garden shows the gratitude sentence, black on white at 14px with 18px lines. `.card-tip.ct-plain` is the shared one.

- **The panel carries a ring, because it has to hold its edge on a PALE card.** On the dark green the sky measured 5.51:1 against the card and needed nothing; on the pale teal it is 1.45, and the panel's own ground band is 1.13, which is no boundary at all. `PANEL_RING` is a 2px line in `rgba(20,73,75,0.40)`, stroked after `ctx.restore()` so the whole width is drawn rather than its inner half, and `panelPath` insets by half the ring so the canvas edge does not cut it. It is a line and not a drop shadow because a shadow needs room outside the panel and the canvas is exactly the size of the slot.
- **Both garden cards paint the garden's own scene behind the flowers, and it is load bearing, not decoration.** The stem colour is `hsl(140,52%,28%)`, which the garden picked to sit on its pale sky; against the darkened personal card it measured **1.24:1** and the stems were simply not there. Rather than give one card its own stem colour, the flowers were given the ground they actually grow out of. `paintSceneBack` and `paintSceneFront` in `index.html` hold it, clipped to a rounded panel by `panelPath`, and one painter serves both cards because the two sketches hold the same six colours.
  - **The panel is a window onto the top 85% of the garden**, since the garden's bottom sixth is foreground hill and nothing else. Every band is the garden's own fraction over 0.85 (`0.56 -> 0.659`, `0.64 -> 0.753`, `0.75 -> 0.882`) and every noise amplitude is its pixel figure over the 765 of a 900 tall garden that this window covers. Change the window fraction and all six numbers move together.
  - **The card's blooms are all one layer and it is the garden's FRONT layer**, so the order is scene, then stems and blooms, then `paintSceneFront`, then the labels. That is `draw()` exactly: a front row stem crosses both hills, its base is buried by the foreground hill, and `drawAllDates()` runs after `drawForegroundHill()` so a label is never buried by the ground its own stem is buried in.
  - **`drawGround`'s `#dff0e8` is not painted here because it is not visible in the garden either.** It fills from 0.805 down and `drawForegroundHill` paints over it from 0.75, so it has never shown on either page.
  - **No sun and no drifting clouds.** The sun is a soft 120px feature in a 1440 wide scene and lands as a yellow blob in a 235 wide one, in the corner the tooltip opens into. The clouds are there but still, because these cards hold a still picture.
  - **A folded card measures zero and every slot is painted whether it is open or not**, so `paintGarden` runs at `h = -22` on a phone. Before the scene existed that was merely pointless; the cloud arcs turn it into a thrown `IndexSizeError` that stops the rest of the paint. `paintGarden` returns early on a non-positive width or height.
  - **The bouquet card gets no panel.** A bouquet has no garden to stand in, and its own paper and ribbon already hold it together on the teal. The row reads as two windows and one object, which is what they are.
- **`drawStem` puts its leaves at 0.42 of the way up, not 0.60.** A card's flower is drawn at the garden's own radius in a frame a third of the garden's height, so its stem is short, and at 0.60 the leaves came out tucked under the bloom's lower petals, which are painted after them. 0.42 is low enough to clear the head and high enough to clear the foreground hill.
- **What `headY` may be is decided by the tooltip, and the window is narrow.** With the box clamped to `TOP_Y` a bloom clears its own head only when `headY > TOP_Y + boxHeight + R`; above `TOP_Y + 5 + 1.5R` the box is no longer clamped and then it always clears. The personal box measures 93px, so its blooms cannot sit higher than about 135 whatever else is wanted, which is why they stand lower than the shared card's at 92 to 102 with its 44px box. Both were dropped to `R: 29`, the bottom of the garden's own 28 to 36, to buy back the stem length that costs. **Measure `offsetHeight` rather than counting rows**: the box is 93px, not the 91 the rows add up to.
- **The bouquet is sized from what it PAINTS, not from its canvas, and nothing is cropped.** A bouquet is drawn in a 400 by 500 box but only fills about 283 by 391 of it, so sizing it by the box counted those empty margins as bouquet and it came out a fifth smaller than it needed to be inside the same frame. `paint()` renders once, measures the painted extent with `paintedExtent()`, scales to fit, renders again, and sets `top` so the painted top lands at the frame's top. What hangs outside the frame is empty margin, and `#bouquetShow` clips it. It went through two rounds of being drawn larger than its box and genuinely cropped, with a mask fading the cut edge, to bring its blooms up to the size the gardens draw. Both failed the same way: the crop ran through the wrap, and a bouquet with its paper cut in half reads as an open envelope with flowers in it. **Do not go back to that.** Blooms land at radius 15 to 26 against the gardens' 30 to 34, which is the accepted price of the thing being legible as a bouquet. Its paper is `butter`, the palette's yellow.
- **Everything on the bouquet card is hit tested from the slot, not the canvas.** `#landingBouquet` keeps `pointer-events: none` so the letter behind it stays reachable: a canvas's box is a rectangle whatever its silhouette, and with events on it swallowed everything but a sliver of the card's right edge. So the slot carries the handlers and tests the pointer against `canvas._blooms`, which the renderer already records in CSS pixels. Hovering a bloom names it and gives its meaning, the same as the finished bouquet and the same as a flower in either garden; hovering the letter opens the note.
- **The letter tucks under the wrap's right edge,** placed from JS against the paper's edge read off the painted canvas rather than from a fixed offset, since that edge moves with the slot's width. About a fifth of it ends up behind the paper; at a third neither line on it could be read. It is behind the canvas at `z-index` 1 against its 2. The note it opens takes the card's own paper colour (`hsl(45,80%,92%)`, `.card-tip.ct-letter`) rather than the gardens' white, so opening it reads as the same object. It is positioned from the **centre** (`left: 50%; margin-left: 12px`), not from the right edge: the numbers were measured off the painted canvas, where the sleeve's right edge runs about 24px inside the tag's left edge, and anchoring to the right instead let a narrower card pull the tag clear of the wrap so it read as propped beside the flowers.
- **The slack goes above the picture, not into the copy.** `.card-art-slot { margin-top: auto }`, and `.garden-card-body` is `flex: 0 0 auto`. Give the slack to the copy instead and whichever card has the least text pushes its own picture down.
- **The title reserves two lines and the paragraph four**, since "The Bouquet Builder" wraps where "The Shared Garden" may not. Without those reservations every row below starts at a different height on each card.
- **`.card-art-slot > canvas` must not set `pointer-events: none`.** It did, and the two garden canvases silently stopped receiving any real pointer event, so nobody could hover a flower. The trap is in how it was checked: `canvas.dispatchEvent(new PointerEvent(...))` bypasses hit testing entirely and runs the handler regardless, so a test written that way passes over a card that is dead to the user. **Verify a hover with `document.elementFromPoint`,** which answers the question that actually matters: what would the cursor land on there.
- **The hint line lives inside the slot**, absolutely positioned along its bottom, and the canvases are drawn 22px short of the slot to clear it. **ON A PHONE IT NEEDS A STRIP OF ITS OWN.** The card is narrower there, the description is hidden and everything closes up, so "Tap or hover a flower" landed on the picture above it and on the button below. The slot takes a bottom margin and the hint drops into that margin, so the strip belongs to the hint and to nothing else. Measured after at 390: 39px of clear ground between the hint and the button, and the hint 30px below the canvas. Hung underneath the slot it would push that card's picture up by its own height and only that card would fall out of line, which is exactly what happened to the slideshow dots it replaced.

The meadows were once full-bleed backdrops behind the copy under a gradient, and the bouquet could not join them there: a bouquet is tall and its paper and ribbon sit at the bottom, exactly where such a gradient is at its most opaque, so the card erased the most recognisable part of its own picture. A lavender spike paints well over twice the height it is given and grows upward from its origin, so `paintBloom` gives it a smaller head and a shorter stem than the round blooms; the meadow scenes that used to fill these slots hit the same thing and had to be retuned for it.

**All three cards run the same three slide preview** in that slot: two steps from that card's own flow, then the thing you end up with. `CARD_SHOWS` in `index.html` holds the two gardens' copy and species; the bouquet card's block further down drives its own slides through `window._startShow`, the loop driver the card block exposes, so both live where their data lives and still keep one rhythm.

- **The step slides are the real screens shrunk, not a design of their own.** Same snow `.gg-card`, same `#0f5132` heading, the personal garden's real question and **all eight** of its real options in the 2 by 2 it lays them out in, with the `#bde0d6` border going to `#26a69a` when picked; its Your Flower card with the italic `Species : meaning` line, the real quote, the rainbow hue strip and the turquoise Plant button; the shared garden's Choose Your Flower title, sub and tile grid, then its confirm card; the builder's cream flower tiles and its letter card on its own paper. Sizes are roughly half, since the slot is about half the width of the card it copies. They are markup, not paint, so the words are real text in the page's own type. **If a garden screen changes, change these with it.**
- **Three slides, not four.** The row runs three cards in parallel and the eye reads across it. A fourth slide is another 3.6 seconds before a card comes back round, so seeing one card whole would take about fourteen, and the three cards would drift out of phase with each other. Two steps and the result is the shape; a third step is more of the same rather than more information.
- **The bloom labels and the tooltip are the gardens' own, to the pixel.** Both gardens draw their first ten flowers at radius 28 to 36 and print white text over a halo of the flower's own colour. The personal garden prints **the whole date**, `08/30/2026`, at 13px with four offsets; the shared garden prints the username at 16px with eight, cut at 11 characters with an ellipsis. Neither size varies by species: scaling the label from `R` shrank lavender's date, which the garden never does, so `paintBloom` scales it from the radius a *round* bloom would get at that canvas height. The tooltips match too: the personal garden stacks the species and meaning in bold, then `Mood:`, `Shaped by:`, `Entry:`, a rule and the date in grey; the shared garden shows the gratitude sentence, black on white. `.card-tip.ct-plain` is the shared one.
- **THE SHARED CARD'S THREE ARE THREE HUES, and putu's peony is the purple in the middle.** It was 320 against rachel's 340, which is one pink drawn twice with the blue tulip doing all the separating; the block's own comment has said "blue, purple, pink" the whole time. It is 285. The point of three is that a shared meadow reads as three different people rather than one person's palette.
- **Two blooms on the last slide, not three.** The date really is wider than the flower it is printed on, in the garden as much as here, so a third bloom put one date against the next. For the same reason each bloom's canvas is wider than its flower and `.cs-row` has no gap: the canvas is the spacing, and cutting it to the bloom clipped the date.
- **Tile canvases are measured from their tile, not from the slot.** Sizing them as the slot minus a guess at the paddings of the slide, the card and the tile ran the third bouquet tile 6px past the edge at 375px. `.cs-tile` and `.cs-btile` also need `min-width: 0`: a canvas carries an intrinsic width, so as a grid item it becomes its column's minimum and `1fr` stops dividing the row evenly, the same trap as a canvas in a flex container.
- **A planted bloom carries its label in the middle of its head,** white over a halo of the flower's own colour, which is exactly how both gardens draw the date and the username. Hovering or tapping pops the meaning, or what that person was grateful for, in a white box pinned to the slot, since a fixed height slot cannot grow a line.
- **Only the flowers are drawn,** one small canvas each, which is what makes hit testing unnecessary. They are sized from the slot at paint time: three fixed 74px blooms plus their gaps came to 270 in the 219 wide slot a phone gives, and the outer two hung over both edges of the card.
- **The bouquet slide holds one example, not a rotation.** The slide already comes and goes on the loop; a bouquet that also swapped between four while it showed would be two slideshows running at once in one small card.

**Every card and the About copy name the ACCOUNT, and that was a gap rather than a polish.** All of it was written before there were accounts, usernames, friends, likes or a history, so the page described three separate places with nothing joining them. The personal card said only that you see it; it now says it follows you between devices and keeps what you have planted. The shared card now says signing in lets you like a flower or write back, and its small print carries the one a day limit. The bouquet card says it can go straight to a friend as well as by link. About gained a fourth paragraph naming what one account actually does, and saying you can skip it entirely.

**The three cards' paragraphs are cut to FOUR LINES and their small print to a chip, and the reason is a bug rather than taste.** `.garden-card-desc` carried `margin-bottom: -0.5rem`, which pulled the small print 9px UP into the paragraph's last line on all three cards at once: a paragraph leaves no 9px of slack under its final line, so the pull came straight out of the text. It is **+0.55rem** now, which measures as a 9px gap; at 0 the chip sat directly on the text with only the line's own leading between them. It was also hiding a second fault, since a paragraph running to five lines grows past the four reserved by `min-height`, and the personal card's body came out 330px against the other two at 302 with the row of pictures no longer level. **Keep every one of the three to four lines, and if the gap under a paragraph ever needs closing, take it off the note's own padding rather than off the paragraph.** The notes are chips now, "Private", "Open to all", "By link or to a friend", because a sentence in a 12px rounded box is not a chip.

**The fourth feature card in About is "Your account", and it replaced "8 Symbolic Species".** That one was the whole flower guide section further down said again in a line, which made it the only card on the page repeating something; the account was the thing nothing on the page mentioned at all. The grid is two by two, so the count must stay at four.

**Reflect, share, give.** The three cards are badged with those verbs and each paragraph opens on one, so the theme reads straight down the row. The privacy facts moved to the small print line under each paragraph rather than being lost.

Note that `html` sets `scroll-behavior: smooth`, so anything that scrolls the page programmatically and then reads `scrollY`, or screenshots, has to wait for the animation or set `scrollBehavior = 'auto'` first. Checking the scroll spy without doing that reports the old section.

~1100 lines with three separate inline `<script>` blocks, opening at lines 103, 724 and 825: the hero meadow canvas (flower family #6 in the sync table), side-nav scroll-spy + flower catalogue modal (`flowerData` at ~line 753), and the card-scene + catalogue canvases (family #7). Both flower families live in this one file and must be edited separately. `styles.css` holds the design tokens (`--cream`, `--sky`, `--teal`, `--darkteal`, `--yellow`, `--lightsky`) that the bouquet page redeclares and the garden pages ignore.

## On a phone

**THE GARDEN'S NAME STARTS BELOW THE ICON ROW ON A PHONE.** Both are positioned from the top of the same screen: the four round controls sit at top 16 and are 34 tall, so they own everything down to 50, and the garden's name was placed at 18 and ran straight underneath them. On a laptop there is width enough that they never meet; on a phone the name is centred across the whole screen and the icons are in it. The name goes to 62 and the line under it to 106, both `!important` since `buildUI()` sets the same `top` inline. The line also needs a WIDTH, not just a cap: the sketch sizes it off a formula reserving 200px either side for the Home pill and the icons, which on a 390 screen leaves 195 and wraps a sentence to four lines, and below the icons there is nothing either side to reserve for.

**The side nav is HIDDEN below 1100px, not shrunk, and 1100 is not a taste.** The rail is 48px circles at `left: 14`, so it owns everything out to 62px, and below 1100 the sections and the footer drop to 2.5rem of side padding, which puts their first character at 42.5 and straight underneath it. At 800px wide its last button sat on the G of "Gratitude Garden" in the footer. 1100 is where that padding rule starts, so the rail leaves exactly when the copy moves into its lane; above it the padding is 5rem and both fit, measured at 23px of clearance. It was 768, and 36px circles at left 8 before that. On a narrow screen the copy runs the full width, so the column sat on top of the hero's paragraph and down the left edge of every section after it. What it offers is a jump between sections, which on a phone is what scrolling already is, so it was paying for itself in covered text and giving nothing back.

**The four round icons FADE WHILE THE PAGE IS MOVING and come back 550ms after it stops**, below 768px only. They are fixed over a page whose copy runs the full width, so a section title passes underneath them on the way past and is cut in half while it does; on a wide screen that never happens, because the content is narrower than the window and the corner is empty.

- **Padding the copy away from them was the other option and it is worse.** The overlap only exists for the second or two a title is level with them, and that fix would cost every phone screen 90px of width for ever.
- **It lives in `garden-cursor.js`**, which owns the chrome every page shares, as one rule keyed off `data-gg-scrolling` on the root element. The gardens do not scroll at all, so it costs those pages nothing either way.

**The page sparkles decide for THEMSELVES whether to draw, from the section's own box, every frame.** The IntersectionObserver is an optimisation and not the authority: it frees a canvas by setting it to zero when its section is far away, and if its callback was ever late, missed or never delivered, `rec.on` stayed false and that canvas stayed zero FOR GOOD, since no resize event fires to correct it. **That is how the sparkles vanished from the whole page**, with no error anywhere. `paint()` now tests the section's rect itself and the observer's flag only lets it release memory.

**`cursor: pointer` must not be set on any BOX this project draws a cursor over.** `cursor` INHERITS, so a value on `.garden-card` beat the flower and turned the pointer into the operating system's hand over a third of the landing page; `.flower-tile` had the same. Both are gone. The card is a link on a phone and the phone block is where that lives now.

**The hero's top padding is 82 on a phone, not 70.** The account and music buttons end at 52 there, and the eyebrow used to start level with them.

**The meadow shows a NARROWER SLICE of its logical scene on a small screen, it does not shrink.** It is drawn in a 1440 wide logical space and scaled to fit, so at 390 the scale was 0.27 and a bloom of radius 17 came out five pixels across. `geom.xk` then squeezes the flowers' x positions by the same ratio in `draw()`, so the row still spans the full width rather than running off the right edge, and `minSpacing` thins whichever end up too close. **`GardenLife.meadow` has to be passed `VIEW_W`, not `LOGICAL_W`**, or the butterflies and motes populate a scene half again as wide as the one on screen.

- **THE SLICE IS DERIVED UNDER 900, NOT STEPPED, and that removed two faults at once.** It was 540 under 620 and 980 under 900. The first left a phone at a scale of 0.72 with 12px blooms, still a row of specks; the second put a cliff at exactly 620 where the scale fell from 1.15 to 0.63 and every flower halved for one pixel of window. `VIEW_W = clamp(round(W / 0.95), 360, 980)` holds the scale at a constant 0.95 across the whole range, so a bloom is 27 to 44 pixels on every phone and every small tablet, which is the size the garden pages draw, and nothing jumps as the window is dragged.
- **`minSpacing` is a FRACTION of the width, 0.155, not a pixel count**, because the blooms are a constant fraction of the width too. The old fixed 104 and 88 were tuned when a phone bloom was 12px across and dropped all but three once the blooms doubled, leaving a third of the meadow bare. At 0.155 a phone keeps six of the eleven, spread from 26px to 349px of a 375 wide screen with 27px of clear ground between the closest pair. The first and last kept are both daisies, which is fine: they land at opposite ends, which is the rule the hero already follows for repeats.
- **The x positions span the full width whatever the slice, and the arithmetic is why.** `xk * scale` is `(VIEW_W/1440) * (W/VIEW_W)`, which is `W/1440` with the slice cancelled out. So changing `VIEW_W` changes only how big things are drawn, never where they sit. If the row ever ends short of the right edge, it is `minSpacing` dropping flowers, not the slice.

**THE HERO CANVAS HAS TO MULTIPLY BY THE DEVICE PIXEL RATIO, and it was the one canvas on the page that did not.** It set `c.width` to the CSS width, so on a phone at a ratio of 3 a 390 wide picture was stretched over 1170 real pixels and every stem and petal edge came out soft. That is the blur. It is capped at 2, past which the memory is real and the gain is not, and the context needs `setTransform(dpr,0,0,dpr,0,0)` before the existing `scale(scale, scale)`.

**A section title is `clamp(2.05rem, 9vw, 2.9rem)` on a phone.** It was `clamp(1.6rem, 6vw, 2.4rem)`, which resolved to 27.2px against a 34px control in the corner and a 16.15px line underneath: the loudest thing on the screen was a button, and the title's ratio to its own subtitle was 1.68 against the desktop's 3.06. It is 35 on a 390 screen now, which clears the control, and the hero went to 10.5vw to stay ahead of it.

**The guide's tiles are SQUARE on a phone**, `aspect-ratio: 1` with the pair centred. The mood line and the chip are already hidden at that width, so a tile is a flower and a name, and left to size itself it came out 130 by 209: a tall box round a round thing.

**The whole CARD is a link on a phone.** A folded card was two taps from the garden it describes, one on the chevron and one on the button at its foot. Tapping the card goes straight there now, and the chevron keeps its own job. Three things are excluded and each for its own reason: the chevron, because that is the fold; the picture, because tapping a bloom in it already pops what that flower meant; and any real link inside, which needs no help. **The `<a>` stays in the markup**, so a keyboard still has a proper link to land on.

**Three things shorten on a phone, and none of them is deleted.** The page is read there as one long column, so anything that is a comfortable paragraph beside a picture becomes a screenful on its own.

- **The hero's subtitle has two versions**, `.wide-only` and `.phone-only`, one shown at a time. Three lines of subtitle is most of a phone screen before anything has happened.
- **About opens on one paragraph** with the other three behind a Read more, using the same `data-fold-phone` hook as the footer. It is the longest unbroken run of prose on the page and it sits second, before anybody has seen anything.
- **Each card's paragraph is hidden**, leaving the badge, the title, the small print and the picture. The picture says more than the four lines did, and the `min-height` reserving those lines goes with them or the card keeps their height and shows nothing in it.

**THE EIGHT CATALOGUE TILES ARE SIZED BY MEASUREMENT, through one solver, and the hand-tuned `cy` switch is gone.** `BLOOM_METRICS` in `index.html` holds every species' painted bounding box at R=100 relative to its own origin, read back by rendering each one alone; `fitBloom(flower, W, H, fillH, fillW)` returns the radius and the origin that put that box at one size, centred.

- **At a uniform radius the eight are nowhere near one size**, which is what the old `R = min(W*0.30, H*0.28)` gave them: the sunflower spans 2.69 of R and the tulip 1.60, so the tulip filled 45 percent of its box beside a sunflower filling 75. Measured after the change, all eight span 62 to 62.5 pixels in a 108 by 77 tile and every one is centred to within 0.3 percent.
- **They are held to their BOUNDING SQUARE, the larger of width and height, not to height alone.** Equalising on height draws the lavender's thin spike as tall as the sunflower's whole disc, and a spike at full height beside a disc at full height reads as the lavender being the bigger flower.
- **Where a species paints is not where its origin is.** The lavender and the lotus grow entirely UPWARD out of theirs, so their `b` is 0; that is what the old per species `cy` switch was compensating for, badly. Centring the painted box does it for all of them at once.
- **The modal uses the same solver**, so the picture in the popup cannot drift from the tile that opened it. It falls back to the old figures only if opened before the third script block has parsed.
- **Re-measure `BLOOM_METRICS` if any species' drawing changes.**

**EVERY DIMENSION IN THE CATALOGUE'S `drawLavender` NOW SCALES WITH R, and it did not.** `pW`, `xO`, the 10px base offset and the tip ellipse were fixed pixel numbers, so only the spike's height followed the radius: shrink R for a phone tile and the thing got shorter while its petals stayed 14px wide, which is the same flower a third fatter. Measured at 32 by 58 on a phone against 32 by 85 on a laptop; it is 23 by 62 now, matching the 0.37 width to height the gardens draw. The ratios are the old numbers over 42, the R the desktop tile gave, so that tile is unchanged. The bouquet's own `drawLavender` carries a note about exactly this trap; this was the seventh copy that still had it. **Change them as a set.**

**A BARE `[data-flower="..."]` SELECTOR IS WRONG ON THIS PAGE.** The flower ring adds a `<button class="fr-bloom" data-flower="...">` for each of the eight, it sits EARLIER in the document than the catalogue canvases, and it carries no `data-hue`. So `openFlowerModal`'s colour lookup returned the ring's button, all three reads fell back to their defaults, and **every one of the eight opened in the same pink**: the green lotus, the blue tulip and the purple lavender all came out at hue 340. Measured across five species before the fix, every modal read 340 to 342 whatever its tile said. Scope it to `canvas.flower-canvas[data-flower="..."]`, and scope anything else that reads a flower's colour out of the page the same way.

**`renderFlowerCanvas` MEASURES the picture box, it does not dictate it.** It used to set the wrap to the tile's full `offsetWidth` and a hard coded 150 tall, INLINE, which beat the stylesheet: once the tiles went square on a phone the CSS asked for a 68px picture and got a 150px one anyway, so the canvas covered the name printed under it. **The wrap's size is the stylesheet's business.**

**The GARDEN TIPS card folds on a phone**, to its own heading with a chevron. On a desktop it sits in empty grass; on a phone the garden is a strip and the card is a paragraph across the bottom third of it. It is wired from `garden-cursor.js` rather than from either sketch, because both gardens build the same card and a copy in each would be a fifth place the same idea is written down. The card is built by a p5 sketch that starts long after that file runs, so it is looked for on a short interval rather than once.

**The BOUQUET BUILDER on a phone: the preview is NOT sticky and the nav moves to a bar at the foot.** Stuck to the top of a single column it pinned a full sized bouquet over the options underneath, so picking a flower meant scrolling past a picture that would not move. `.bq-sticky-bar` carries the step's own count and its two buttons instead, **by reading them rather than reimplementing them**: it copies the words and the disabled state off the step's real Back and Continue and clicks them, and a MutationObserver on the step keeps the two in step, so a gate added to any step is honoured here for free.

- **THERE IS NO PREVIEW IN THE COLUMN ON A PHONE. It is a button in the bar.** Three arrangements were tried and each failed its own way: pinned and full sized covered the options, pinned and short still sat over whatever scrolled under it, and left in the flow it went off the top so every change meant scrolling back. The answer is that a 375px column cannot hold two things at once, so the picture moved OUT of it. `.bq-peek` opens the bouquet full size over everything from an eye button at the left of the foot bar, and paints on open, since a canvas in a hidden box measures zero.
- **The bar asks the PAGE which step is live rather than trusting the name it was called with.** The mode chooser and the template gallery are shown by routes that do not always go through `goToStep`, so a bar left visible by an earlier step stayed up over both: a Back and a Continue across the bottom of a screen that has neither. It is also settled once at boot for the same reason.
- **`.bq-card` reserves 104px at the foot, not 88.** The bar is about 64 tall before the phone's own home indicator, and at 88 the last row of the template and material grids sat behind it.
- **A TEMPLATE IS A SHORTCUT, SO BACK UNDOES THE SHORTCUT.** Walking backwards out of a template dropped you into the wrap step of a bouquet you never built, which reads as the template having been a starting point you are now editing. `cameFromTemplate` sends the letter step's Back to the gallery instead, and is cleared by "Build your own" so a hand built bouquet still walks its steps.
- **The preview was a SHORT STRIP PINNED TO THE TOP for one round, and it is recorded here only so it is not tried again.** It was sticky and full sized first, which pinned most of a screen of bouquet over the options; it was then unpinned, which sent it up the page out of sight and left you scrolling back and forth to see what a change had done. 118px never leaves and costs about a seventh of a phone screen. It needs `order: -1` and a flex column, since sticking to the top of the viewport is no use to something laid out below the thing it describes.
- **The count note has a `data-short` for the bar.** "2 picked, the most you can mix" is right on the step, which has the width of the card; in the foot bar it shares a 375px row with a Back and a Continue and wrapped to three lines. The bar reads `data-short` when it is there and the full text when it is not, so a step that sets no short form still works.
- **The template grid stays at TWO columns all the way down**, where it used to fall to one under 480. Six templates in a single column is six full width tiles with most of them below the fold, so the choice reads as one option followed by a lot of scrolling.
- **The bar asks whether the reveal is VISIBLE, not what the step is called.** Testing the name alone was not enough: a received bouquet and `renderFinal` both put the reveal on screen without going through `goToStep`, which left the letter step's Back and "Reveal my bouquet" stuck across the bottom of a finished bouquet, on top of the share buttons that share that space. `renderFinal` hides the bar itself for the same reason.
- **Those overrides live at the END of `bouquet-style.css`, and they have to.** `.bq-preview-col` and `.bq-nav` are both declared after the 780px block that used to hold them, and at equal specificity the later rule wins: written in the earlier block they were quietly undone a hundred lines later.

**The footer's two blocks are `<details open>` that CLOSE on a phone.** The `open` attribute is in the markup on purpose: a details element with no `open` arrives shut before any stylesheet can say otherwise, which would hide the page's only navigation at the foot of a desktop view. A small script closes everything carrying `data-fold-phone` under 768 and reopens it above, and **stops doing either the moment somebody opens or closes one by hand**, so a rotation cannot undo a reader's choice. They also need `width: 100%` there, since as flex items they were shrink to fit and the chevron sat against the heading instead of out at the edge.

## The controls all live in one corner

**Every page's controls are one row of round icons in the top right**, 38px each with an 8px gap, so the slots are `right: 16, 62, 108, 154`.

| slot | control | built by |
|---|---|---|
| 16 | music | `garden-music.js` |
| 62 | account and friends | `garden-account.js` |
| 108 | history | `garden-journal.js` |
| 154 | save a picture | each garden's own sketch |

- **The history was a labelled pill in the BOTTOM LEFT and sat straight on top of the Garden Tips card.** That corner is the tips card's, on both gardens.
- **Save PNG was the only labelled control up there**, so it is a camera now. `#save-btn` is set inline by the sketch AND in the stylesheet; both carry the same numbers so the dead rule cannot resurface with the old pill look. It shows as `flex`, not `block`, or the icon does not centre.
- **The personal garden's bottom right `#gg-account` chip is gone** when `garden-account.js` is live, because that module's panel already shows the email and carries Sign out. Two places to sign out is one too many, and it also sat exactly where `#gg-logo` would reappear if the `LOGO-01.png` 404 were ever fixed.
- **The account icon has no written label, so its state is in `aria-label` and its hover tip**: Sign in, or Friends with the username. The pending count rides the icon's corner.
- **NONE of the four carries a `title`.** A `title` beside a `data-tip` means hovering produces the project's own label and then the operating system's on top of it. See "The hover labels" below.

## `garden-cursor.js` owns the chrome every page shares

Two things live in this one file, and they are together because both are needed on all four pages and neither belongs to any one of them: the cursor, and the hover label on an icon.

### The hover labels

**Any control carrying `data-tip` gets a short label under it.** The browser's own `title` already does this, but only after a long pause and in the operating system's styling, and the top right of every page is now a row of four unlabelled icons, which is exactly where a fast legible answer to "what is this" is worth having. It is pure CSS off an attribute, so a module adds one by setting a single attribute and nothing has to be built.

- **It is CENTRED on its button**, `left: 50%` with a `translateX(-50%)`. Anchored to a corner it pointed at the gap between two icons rather than at the one it belonged to, since these are round icons in a row eight pixels apart.
- **The music button has NO label, and that is the one exception.** Its volume panel drops into exactly the space a centred label would take, and pushed to the left instead it ran across the three icons beside it. It is also the one button in the row that explains itself, since hovering it slides out a volume slider. `data-tip-side="left"` is gone with it; nothing uses it.
- **None of these buttons carries a `title` any more.** `title` and `data-tip` together meant hovering produced the project's own label and then the operating system's on top of it, which is what "don't have it doubled" was. `aria-label` is what a screen reader reads and it is enough. **Do not add a `title` to anything that has a `data-tip`.**
- **`@media (hover: none)` removes it.** A label stuck open after a tap is worse than none.

### The cursor

**A FLAT FIVE PETALLED FLOWER at rest, shrinking to a SPARKLE over anything clickable, on ALL FOUR pages.** It used to live in `bouquet-style.css` and appear on one page of four, which made that page the odd one out rather than a theme.

- **THE FLOWER IS AT REST AND THE SPARKLE IS ON A CONTROL, which is the reverse of where it started.** A solid bloom over a control sits on top of the very thing being aimed at, at the moment its hover label is opening and wanting to be read. At rest there is nothing underneath to hide, so the rose can be the size it needs to read as a rose, and the sparkle that replaces it is small and mostly empty. **Do not swap them back without solving the covering.**
- **A CURSOR CAN CARRY ABOUT SIX SHAPES. Anything past that is texture nobody can see and weight everybody pays.** This is the rule the whole history of this file comes down to. It has been the favicon's concentric scalloped rings, then a rose with a spiral centre inside three rounds of overlapping cupped petals, eighteen shapes in all. At thirty pixels that was a blue smudge, and it did not read as a rose either. **Do not add layers, shading or a spiral back.**
- **So it is one ring of five petals, one flat blue, one outline, one centre.** `flowerArt()`, and that is the whole drawing. It is what a flower looks like at icon size and it still reads at twenty, which is what the smaller of the two states needs.
- **Its blue is the favicon's**, sampled off `apple-touch-icon.png`: hue 206 at about 58 percent saturation, pushed here to 66 because an icon sits in browser chrome of its own while this has to hold against six section colours. The flower is no longer the favicon's SHAPE, only its colour.
- **The DOUBLE OUTLINE is the only thing that works on every ground, and it was measured.** No single outline colour can do it: dark teal scores 1.00 against the dark teal band and 1.35 against the dark green card, and cream scores 1.00 against cream and 1.06 against the pale blue. A near black line inside a cream halo always has one of the two separating, 13.2 on cream and 6.5 on dark teal. The sparkle carries the same treatment for the same reason.
- **EVERY halo is stroked before ANY petal is filled.** Interleaved, a later petal's cream stroke lands on top of an earlier petal's fill and cuts a pale scar across the flower.
- **206 also does something no other hue here can.** Every colour on these pages is a teal, a cream or a pastel bloom, so a saturated blue is the one thing never mistaken for the page behind it.
- **Things tried and rejected, so they are not tried again:** a leaf (the one thing in a project of flowers that was not a flower); the same flower at two sizes (on pale cream the colour barely moved and a size step that small reads as the pointer wobbling); a bud drawn as five narrow petals tucked in (overlapping ellipses at that size read as a spindly star); and a plain teal arrow, accurate and with nothing to do with the project.
- **`DEFAULT_HUE` is 206 and `apply()` now moves BOTH states.** The rose is the one you actually look at, so in the personal garden it is the rose that carries the hue of the flower planted most recently; tinting only the little sparkle would have put the personal touch on the half nobody studies.
- **The rose is 30 pixels and NOTHING HERE MAY EXCEED 32.** Windows refuses a custom cursor larger than 32 by 32 outright, and a refused cursor falls back to the plain system arrow, so anything bigger silently loses the mark on every Windows machine.
- **The FALLBACK keyword differs by state**, `auto` at rest and `pointer` on a clickable. It is what a machine that refuses to draw a custom cursor gets, and giving both the same one would leave such a machine with no signal at all.
- **It is injected from JS rather than written in a stylesheet** so the bloom can be RECOLOURED at runtime. In the personal garden it takes the hue of the flower planted most recently, so the thing following the pointer is the last thing that grew. Everywhere else it is the palette's butter yellow.
- **EVERY rule carries `!important`, and that is a fix rather than a shortcut.** This file is loaded from the head, so its style tag is the FIRST in the document, and every module that builds a control appends its own tag after it. Four of them set `cursor: pointer` on an ID (`#gj-btn`, `#gg-music-btn`, `#ga-friends-btn`, `#gj-close`), which beats an element selector outright, and both gardens set `#save-btn`'s cursor INLINE, which nothing but `!important` reaches. So the four round icons in the corner of every page kept the system arrow while everything around them drew a flower. **Adding those ids to the list would NOT have worked**: each selector in a list carries its own specificity, so `#gj-btn` there would only tie, and the module's tag comes later.
- **The class list is still load bearing.** A class rule carrying `cursor: pointer` outranks a bare `button` selector, so every clickable class in the project is named explicitly. Anything new that sets its own `cursor: pointer` has to be added there.
- **What is NOT in that list matters as much, and three entries were removed.** `.garden-card-inner` put the flower over the whole of each of the three cards, six hundred pixels of title, paragraph and small print, when the only thing to press is the button at its foot. `.flower-tile` did the same across each guide tile. `label[for]` is clickable in the technical sense and in no other. All three still work exactly as before; they just show the arrow while you read them. **The rule is that the flower marks a CONTROL, and a large box you happen to be able to click is not one.**
- **Inputs, sliders and disabled controls are declared LAST so they win**, and they carry `!important` too, so they still win by order and by specificity. A caret and a grab handle each say something true about a control that a decorative cursor would throw away.
- **`@media (hover: none)` turns the whole thing off**, since a touch screen has no cursor to draw.

## The flower guide is a RING, and the grid beside it is the fallback

**The eight blooms drawn close up and set round a circle, with a hairline running out of whichever one is under the pointer to its own name and meaning.** It is a botanical plate: the specimen in the middle and the naming off to the side on a leader line, rather than a caption sitting under a picture in a box.

- **There was briefly a display heading and a numbers band here and both are GONE.** The heading mixed an italic serif drop capital with a sans second line, and the band carried four figures about the project. They came from a reference photograph and read as another site's furniture dropped onto this one. The heading is back to `Every mood has a <em>symbolic bloom</em>` and the band and all of its CSS are deleted. **Do not bring either back**; the thing that was wanted from that reference was the annotation lines, which is what the ring is.
- **The ring is ROTATED by half a step**, so the eight sit at 22.5, 67.5, 112.5 and 157.5 degrees either side rather than on the compass points. That is not decoration: a bloom at the very top has no side to put a label on, and one at the far left has no room above or below it. Off the axes every one of the eight has an unambiguous side and a clear run out to it.
- **Nothing in the geometry is a percentage.** A label's own measured width is the budget the ring lives inside, so `layoutRing` reads `offsetWidth` back and sizes the ring to what is left. The bloom's size and the radius each depend on the other, so it is solved in two passes rather than guessed.
- **The stage's height is measured, not set.** It comes out of the extreme label and bloom positions, so a long meaning can never run out of the section it annotates.
- **The ring IS A COLOUR WHEEL, and the hues were moved to make it one.** It used to carry the rose, the lotus and the sakura all at hue 340, three flowers in one pink sitting side by side, with a 140 degree jump from the sunflower straight to the lily. Clockwise from the top left it now runs rose 350, daisy 25, sunflower 48, lotus 140, lily 190, tulip 232, lavender 272, sakura 332, back to the rose: every step climbs and no two are closer than 18 degrees. None of the eight sits on a compass point either, which is what gives every one a side to put its label on.
- **THE LOTUS IS THE ONE THAT WENT GREEN, at hue 140, and it is the only one of the three pinks that could.** A rose has to be red and a cherry blossom has to be pink; a lotus comes in white and cream and a pale green white, so a soft sage one is a flower that exists. Its saturation is held down to 34, or it stops reading as a bloom and starts reading as a leaf. **Its position moved with its colour**, from beside the rose at the top to between the sunflower and the lily, which is where a green belongs on a wheel and is also the one stretch that had nothing in it.
- **The 92 degree step from the sunflower to the lotus is the yellow green arc, and it stays a gap on purpose.** None of the eight is a yellow green flower, and the leaves already are.
- **The blooms are `Rr * 0.85`, floored at 118 and capped at 176.** The chord between two neighbours is 0.765 of the radius and a bloom paints about four fifths of its square, so at this factor there is still air between them: measured at radius 140 the chord is 107 against painted blooms of 95.
- **TWO of the eight hues were moved to pull neighbours apart, and both live in `index.html` TWICE**: the `RING` array and the tile canvas's `data-hue`, which the popup also reads its colour from. The tulip was 280, four degrees off the lavender's 270, so the ring carried the same purple twice side by side; it is 232, between the lavender and the lily and still on the violet side of blue. The daisy was 55, three degrees off the sunflower's 45 with the same yellow; it is 22, between the sakura and the sunflower, which makes it the ring's one apricot. **The bouquet's own `SPECIES` palette is separate and was NOT touched**, since a hue there is the default for every bouquet ever built.
- **THE HEADING SITS ON TOP, like every other section's, and that is settled.** It was in the middle of the ring for a while. Distinctive, and wrong: all five other sections open with an eyebrow and a title near the top, landing within 75px of each other, so the flower guide hiding its title inside its content was the one section announcing itself differently and it read as belonging to another site. It cost as well, because type in the middle needs a wide empty circle, a wide circle needs a big radius, and a big radius made the section a screen and a third. **Do not put it back without re-reading this.**
- **Every bloom's ORIGIN in the ring is placed by MEASUREMENT, not by the catalogue tiles' numbers.** Where a species' origin goes is not where its middle ends up: each paints a different amount above and below it, and the lavender and the lotus grow upward out of an origin their own drawing pushes down. Painted and read back, the vertical centre landed at 0.519 of the slot for most of them, 0.635 for the tulip, 0.622 for the lavender and 0.461 for the lotus, against a middle of 0.5, which is the tulip sitting an eighth of its box low beside the lily. Each origin is shifted by its own error and all eight now land within 0.001. **Re-measure if any species' drawing changes.**
- **The eight stand on a faint DASHED CIRCLE**, added to the svg before anything else so every leader line and every bloom paints over it. Without it they read as eight flowers that happen to be scattered; with it the arrangement is plainly a diagram, which is what the labels are already pretending to be. It is the ruling on the page rather than a thing to look at: 1px, `rgba(66,146,136,0.30)`, dashed 2 on 7.
- **It is a CIRCLE with its radius CAPPED at 215, not solved from the width.** The height follows the radius directly, so letting it take all the width available runs the section past a screen and a half: at a 1240 box the width alone gives 403 and the section would be 1.58 screens. 215 keeps it at about 1.2, and the room that leaves at the sides is where the two corner notes sit. It was briefly an ELLIPSE, with the vertical radius capped and the horizontal one carrying on, which filled the sides with flowers instead of text; the notes do that job now.
- **The two SIDE NOTES flank the circle at MID HEIGHT**, one either side, turned inward: the left one is right aligned and the right one left aligned, so both read toward the diagram. They were in the top left and bottom right corners first, which is where the page is emptiest but also furthest from anything; the top one ended eighty pixels above the ring even began, and both read as stray paragraphs rather than as notes on it.
